# RFC 9110 (HTTP Semantics) + RFC 9112 (HTTP/1.1 Message Syntax), June 2022 -- Current HTTP/1.1 Normative Facts Catalog [2026 slice, supersedes RFC 2616]

_Generated from local RFC source via FABLE extraction + double independent Opus verification. Every quote is verbatim from the fetched RFC text; two independent verifiers re-read the source and confirmed quote/level/applicability (default-refute). Rows flagged VERIFY had a verifier disagreement and need a manual source check. Sections 6 and 7 exist in both RFCs and are disambiguated by extract title + verifier count._

### RFC 9112 1-2 - Introduction, Message Format, Message Parsing, HTTP Version

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9112 1-2-1` | informative | BCP 14 keywords are normative only when they appear in all capitals. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 [RFC2119] [RFC8174] when, and only when, they appear in all capitals | Treat only ALL-CAPS MUST/SHOULD/MAY in RFC text as conformance-gating when mapping tests in RFC_COVERAGE.md. |
| `9112 1-2-2` | informative | Conformance criteria and error-handling considerations are inherited from RFC 9110 Section 2. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Conformance criteria and considerations regarding error handling are defined in Section 2 of [HTTP]. | Apply RFC 9110 §2 conformance/error-handling rules (e.g., recipient may be tolerant, sender must be strict) across the http1 package. |
| `9112 1-2-3` | informative | Grammar is ABNF per RFC 5234 extended with RFC 7405 case-sensitive string notation. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | This specification uses the Augmented Backus-Naur Form (ABNF) notation of [RFC5234], extended with the notation for case-sensitivity in strings defined in [RFC7405]. | Interpret %s"..." strings (e.g., HTTP-name) as byte-exact case-sensitive; plain quoted ABNF strings as case-insensitive. |
| `9112 1-2-4` | informative | The "#" ABNF operator denotes comma-separated lists as defined in RFC 9110 Section 5.6.1. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a list extension, defined in Section 5.6.1 of [HTTP], that allows for compact definition of comma-separated lists using a "#" operator | When implementing any #rule field (e.g., Transfer-Encoding, Connection), parse/emit as comma-separated list with OWS per RFC 9110 §5.6.1. |
| `9112 1-2-5` | informative | ABNF rule names prefixed "obs-" are obsolete grammar retained for historical reasons. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | ABNF rule names prefixed with "obs-" denote obsolete grammar rules that appear for historical reasons. | Never emit obs- constructs (obs-fold, obs-text); decide a tolerant-receive policy for each and document it. |
| `9112 1-2-6` | informative | Core byte-class rules (ALPHA, CR, CRLF, CTL, DIGIT, DQUOTE, HEXDIG, HTAB, LF, OCTET, SP, VCHAR) come from RFC 5234 Appendix B.1. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The following core rules are included by reference, as defined in [RFC5234], Appendix B.1: ALPHA (letters), CR (carriage return), CRLF (CR LF), CTL (controls), DIGIT (decimal 0-9) | Byte-classification tables in the parser must match RFC 5234 core rule definitions exactly (e.g., HTAB=0x09, SP=0x20, VCHAR=0x21-0x7E). |
| `9112 1-2-7` | informative | token, quoted-string, obs-text, field-name, field-value, OWS/RWS/BWS, absolute-path, and transfer-coding are imported by reference from RFC 9110. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | obs-text = <obs-text, see [HTTP], Section 5.6.4> quoted-string = <quoted-string, see [HTTP], Section 5.6.4> token = <token, see [HTTP], Section 5.6.2> | Implement token/quoted-string/field-name/field-value/OWS validators against RFC 9110 §5 definitions, not local approximations; header names must be tokens. |
| `9112 1-2-8` | informative | absolute-URI, authority, uri-host, port, and query grammar are imported by reference from RFC 3986. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | absolute-URI = <absolute-URI, see [URI], Section 4.3> authority = <authority, see [URI], Section 3.2> uri-host = <host, see [URI], Section 3.2.2> | Request-target components the client emits (origin-form path, absolute-URI for proxy, authority for CONNECT) must satisfy RFC 3986 grammar. |
| `9112 1-2-9` | informative | An HTTP/1.1 message is a start-line, zero or more header field lines, an empty line ending the header section, and an optional body. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | zero or more header field lines (collectively referred to as the "headers" or the "header section"), an empty line indicating the end of the header section, and an optional message body | Serializer writes request-line, each field line, then empty CRLF before body; response parser treats empty line as end of header section. |
| `9112 1-2-10` | informative | Grammar: HTTP-message = start-line CRLF *( field-line CRLF ) CRLF [ message-body ]. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | HTTP-message = start-line CRLF *( field-line CRLF ) CRLF [ message-body ] | Every line of the emitted request terminates with CRLF (not bare LF); parser requires this framing structure for responses (modulo the bare-LF tolerance in 1-2-15). |
| `9112 1-2-11` | informative | Grammar: start-line = request-line / status-line; requests and responses differ syntactically only in the start-line and body-length algorithm. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | start-line = request-line / status-line | Client emits request-line as start-line and parses status-line as start-line; body-length determination differs per role (RFC 9112 §6). |
| `9112 1-2-12` | informative | In practice clients are implemented to expect only responses on a connection (servers only requests). <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | servers are implemented to only expect a request (a response is interpreted as an unknown or invalid request method), and clients are implemented to only expect a response | Client-side parser only needs a status-line parser; bytes not matching status-line grammar are a parse error, not a request. |
| `9112 1-2-13` | informative | Normal parsing reads start-line, then field lines by name until the empty line, then reads the body as a stream until the computed length is consumed or the connection closes. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | it is read as a stream until an amount of octets equal to the message body length is read or the connection is closed | Response body reader consumes exactly the computed message-body length (or until close for close-delimited bodies); header fields collected keyed by field name. |
| `9112 1-2-14` | MUST | A recipient must parse HTTP messages as octet sequences in a US-ASCII-superset encoding, never as Unicode character streams. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST parse an HTTP message as a sequence of octets in an encoding that is a superset of US-ASCII [USASCII]. | Parse on raw []byte; never decode the wire bytes to string/UTF-8 before field lines are delineated (Go []byte parsing satisfies this — verify no early string conversion). |
| `9112 1-2-15` | MAY | A recipient may accept a single LF as a line terminator, ignoring any preceding CR. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a recipient MAY recognize a single LF as a line terminator and ignore any preceding CR | Optional tolerance: response parser may split lines on LF and strip a trailing CR; if adopted, document and test it; if not, bare-LF responses are invalid. |
| `9112 1-2-16` | MUST NOT | A sender must never emit a bare CR (CR not followed by LF) in any protocol element outside the content. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A sender MUST NOT generate a bare CR (a CR character not immediately followed by LF) within any protocol elements other than the content. | Reject or sanitize CR in caller-supplied method, target, field names/values before serialization; only body bytes may contain lone CR. |
| `9112 1-2-17` | MUST | A recipient of a bare CR must treat the element as invalid or replace each bare CR with SP before processing or forwarding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient of such a bare CR MUST consider that element to be invalid or replace each bare CR with SP before processing the element or forwarding the message. | Response parser must detect lone CR inside status-line/field-lines and either error the message or substitute SP — it must not pass bare CR through untouched. |
| `9112 1-2-18` | MUST NOT | An HTTP/1.1 user agent must not send an extra CRLF before or after a request. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | An HTTP/1.1 user agent MUST NOT preface or follow a request with an extra CRLF. | Serializer emits no leading CRLF before the request-line and no trailing CRLF after the message body; assert wire bytes in tests. |
| `9112 1-2-19` | MUST | If a request body is deliberately terminated with a line-ending, the user agent must count those CRLF octets in the message body length. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | the user agent MUST count the terminating CRLF octets as part of the message body length | Content-Length (or chunk size) must include any trailing CRLF the caller put in the body; the client never appends CRLF outside the declared length. |
| `9112 1-2-20` | SHOULD | A server expecting a request-line should ignore at least one empty line (CRLF) received before it. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | a server that is expecting to receive and parse a request-line SHOULD ignore at least one empty line (CRLF) received prior to the request-line | Server-only robustness; no client parser obligation (and per 1-2-18 the client must not rely on it by sending extra CRLF). |
| `9112 1-2-21` | MUST NOT | A sender must not put whitespace between the start-line and the first header field. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A sender MUST NOT send whitespace between the start-line and the first header field. | Serializer writes the first field line immediately after request-line CRLF with no leading SP/HTAB; reject field names starting with whitespace. |
| `9112 1-2-22` | MUST | A recipient seeing whitespace between the start-line and the first header field must reject the message or consume (ignore) each whitespace-preceded line until a proper field or end of headers. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | MUST either reject the message as invalid or consume each whitespace-preceded line without further processing of it | Response parser hitting a SP/HTAB-prefixed line directly after the status-line must error the response or skip that line and all subsequent whitespace-prefixed lines — never interpret it as a field (response-splitting defense). |
| `9112 1-2-23` | SHOULD | A server receiving octets not matching the HTTP-message grammar (beyond the listed robustness exceptions) should respond 400 and close the connection. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server SHOULD respond with a 400 (Bad Request) response and close the connection | Server-only; client side just needs to tolerate a 400-plus-close in reply to a malformed request. |
| `9112 1-2-24` | informative | The message version is carried in the start-line's HTTP-version field, which is case-sensitive. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The version of an HTTP/1.x message is indicated by an HTTP-version field in the start-line. HTTP-version is case-sensitive. | Emit exactly "HTTP/1.1"; compare received version bytes case-sensitively — "http/1.1" or "Http/1.1" in a status-line is invalid. |
| `9112 1-2-25` | informative | Grammar: HTTP-version = HTTP-name "/" DIGIT "." DIGIT, with HTTP-name = %s"HTTP" (case-sensitive). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | HTTP-version = HTTP-name "/" DIGIT "." DIGIT HTTP-name = %s"HTTP" | Version parser accepts exactly uppercase "HTTP", slash, single digit, dot, single digit (so "HTTP/1.10" or "HTTP/01.1" is malformed); serializer hardcodes "HTTP/1.1". |
| `9112 1-2-26` | informative | A conformant sender uses only HTTP/1.0-compatible features until it has determined (by configuration or a received message) that the recipient supports HTTP/1.1. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a conformant sender will only use compatible features until it has determined, through configuration or the receipt of a message, that the recipient supports HTTP/1.1 | Client must not depend on 1.1-only features (e.g., chunked requests, 100-continue) toward a peer known/observed to be HTTP/1.0; track peer version from received status-lines. |
| `9112 1-2-27` | MUST | Message-processing intermediaries (all except tunnels) must send their own HTTP-version in forwarded messages, unless purposefully downgrading for an upstream issue. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | MUST send their own HTTP-version in forwarded messages, unless it is purposefully downgraded as a workaround for an upstream issue | Intermediary-only; not applicable to poseidon's client role (it never forwards messages). |
| `9112 1-2-28` | MAY | A server may answer an HTTP/1.1 request with an HTTP/1.0 response when the client is known or suspected to mis-implement version handling. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY send an HTTP/1.0 response to an HTTP/1.1 request if it is known or suspected that the client incorrectly implements the HTTP specification | Client parser must accept an HTTP/1.0 status-line in reply to an HTTP/1.1 request and adjust behavior (no persistent-connection default, no chunked expectation). |
| `9112 1-2-29` | SHOULD NOT | Servers should not perform such protocol downgrades unless triggered by specific client attributes (e.g., a matching User-Agent known to be in error). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Such protocol downgrades SHOULD NOT be performed unless triggered by specific client attributes | Server-only policy; no client implementation obligation beyond 1-2-28 tolerance. |

### RFC 9112 3 - Request Line: Method, Request Target, Reconstructing the Target URI

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9112 3-1` | informative | A request-line is the method, a single SP, the request-target, a single SP, and the HTTP version. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | request-line = method SP request-target SP HTTP-version | Emit exactly `method SP request-target SP HTTP-version` with one SP octet between elements, terminated by CRLF; no other separators. |
| `9112 3-2` | MAY | Recipients may leniently parse the request-line on whitespace-delimited word boundaries, treating SP/HTAB/VT/FF/bare-CR runs as the SP separator. <br>_(server,proxy [not-client])_ | recipients MAY instead parse on whitespace-delimited word boundaries and, aside from the CRLF terminator, treat any form of whitespace as the SP separator | Server-side parsing option only; client must never rely on it — always emit single SP (lenient parsing enables request smuggling). |
| `9112 3-3` | informative | HTTP defines no predefined upper limit on request-line length; limits are implementation policy. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | HTTP does not place a predefined limit on the length of a request-line | Do not hardcode an arbitrary send-side cap; treat any rejection of long request-lines as server policy (414/501), not protocol. |
| `9112 3-4` | SHOULD | A server receiving a method longer than any it implements should respond 501 (Not Implemented). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that receives a method longer than any that it implements SHOULD respond with a 501 (Not Implemented) status code. | No emit-side duty; client should surface a 501 cleanly when sending unusual methods. |
| `9112 3-5` | MUST | A server receiving a request-target longer than any URI it wishes to parse must respond 414 (URI Too Long). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that receives a request-target longer than any URI it wishes to parse MUST respond with a 414 (URI Too Long) status code | Client must handle a 414 response for long targets; do not retry unchanged. |
| `9112 3-6` | RECOMMENDED | All HTTP senders and recipients are recommended to support request-lines of at least 8000 octets. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | It is RECOMMENDED that all HTTP senders and recipients support, at a minimum, request-line lengths of 8000 octets. | Client's request serializer must build and send request-lines up to at least 8000 octets without truncating or erroring. |
| `9112 3-7` | informative | The method is grammatically a token. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | method = token | Validate the caller-supplied method against token grammar (RFC 9110 tchar) before emitting; reject anything with delimiters/whitespace. |
| `9112 3-8` | informative | The request method is case-sensitive. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The request method is case-sensitive. | Never case-normalize the method; emit exactly the bytes provided ("GET" != "get"). |
| `9112 3-9` | informative | The request-target has exactly four forms: origin-form, absolute-form, authority-form, asterisk-form. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | request-target = origin-form / absolute-form / authority-form / asterisk-form | Request-target builder must select exactly one of the four forms based on method (CONNECT/OPTIONS) and whether the request goes to a proxy. |
| `9112 3-10` | informative | Whitespace is forbidden inside the request-target. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | No whitespace is allowed in the request-target. | Reject or percent-encode SP/HTAB/CR/LF and any whitespace in the target URI before emitting the request-line. |
| `9112 3-11` | SHOULD | Recipients of an invalid request-line should respond 400 or a 301 redirect with the target properly encoded. <br>_(server,proxy [not-client])_ | Recipients of an invalid request-line SHOULD respond with either a 400 (Bad Request) error or a 301 (Moved Permanently) redirect with the request-target properly encoded. | Server duty; client-side corollary is to never emit an invalid request-line, and to handle a 301 whose Location re-encodes the target. |
| `9112 3-12` | SHOULD NOT | A recipient should not autocorrect an invalid request-line and process it without a redirect. <br>_(server,proxy [not-client])_ | A recipient SHOULD NOT attempt to autocorrect and then process the request without a redirect | Server duty (anti-smuggling); no client emit-side action. |
| `9112 3-13` | MUST | A client must send a Host header field in every HTTP/1.1 request message. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST send a Host header field (Section 7.2 of [HTTP]) in all HTTP/1.1 request messages. | Unconditionally add Host to every request the client serializes; there is no code path that may omit it. |
| `9112 3-14` | MUST | If the target URI has an authority component, the Host field value must be identical to it, minus any userinfo and its "@". <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a client MUST send a field value for Host that is identical to that authority component, excluding any userinfo subcomponent and its "@" delimiter | Derive Host verbatim from the URI authority (host[:port]); strip userinfo@ if present; do not re-case or re-encode. |
| `9112 3-15` | MUST | If the target URI's authority component is missing or undefined, the client must send a Host header with an empty field value. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If the authority component is missing or undefined for the target URI, then a client MUST send a Host header field with an empty field value. | For authority-less URIs emit `Host:` with empty value rather than omitting the field. |
| `9112 3-16` | MUST | A server must respond 400 to any HTTP/1.1 request lacking Host, containing more than one Host field line, or containing an invalid Host value. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST respond with a 400 (Bad Request) status code to any HTTP/1.1 request message that lacks a Host header field | Server duty, but the client must guarantee exactly one valid Host field per request or every conforming server will 400 it — check for duplicate Host injection via user-supplied headers. |
| `9112 3-17` | informative | origin-form is an absolute path optionally followed by "?" and a query. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | origin-form = absolute-path [ "?" query ] | Origin-form serializer must output a path starting with "/" plus optional "?query"; no scheme, authority, or fragment. |
| `9112 3-18` | MUST | For direct-to-origin requests (other than CONNECT or server-wide OPTIONS), the client must send only the absolute path and query of the target URI. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a client MUST send only the absolute path and query components of the target URI as the request-target | When not talking to a proxy, strip scheme+authority and send path?query only; authority goes in Host. |
| `9112 3-19` | MUST | If the target URI's path component is empty, the client must send "/" as the origin-form path. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If the target URI's path component is empty, the client MUST send "/" as the path within the origin-form of request-target. | Map empty path to "/" (e.g. "http://example.com" becomes "GET / HTTP/1.1"); never emit an empty request-target. |
| `9112 3-20` | MUST | When making a request to a proxy (other than CONNECT or server-wide OPTIONS), the client must send the target URI in absolute-form. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a client MUST send the target URI in "absolute-form" as the request-target | If the transport goes through a configured HTTP proxy, serialize the full absolute-URI as the request-target instead of origin-form. |
| `9112 3-21` | informative | absolute-form is an absolute-URI. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | absolute-form = absolute-URI | Absolute-form serializer emits scheme://authority/path?query (absolute-URI grammar: no fragment). |
| `9112 3-22` | MUST | A client must send a Host header even when the request-target is in absolute-form. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST send a Host header field in an HTTP/1.1 request even if the request-target is in the absolute-form | Do not suppress Host on proxied absolute-form requests; emit both the absolute-URI target and a matching Host field. |
| `9112 3-23` | MUST | A proxy receiving an absolute-form request must ignore the received Host and replace it with the host information from the request-target. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | the proxy MUST ignore the received Host header field (if any) and instead replace it with the host information of the request-target | Proxy-only duty; client just needs to know its request-target (not Host) is authoritative when going through a proxy. |
| `9112 3-24` | MUST | A proxy forwarding an absolute-form request must generate a new Host value from the received request-target rather than forwarding the received Host. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy that forwards such a request MUST generate a new Host field value based on the received request-target rather than forward the received Host field value. | Proxy-only duty; no client action. |
| `9112 3-25` | MUST | An origin server receiving an absolute-form request must ignore the received Host and use the host information of the request-target. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | the origin server MUST ignore the received Host header field (if any) and instead use the host information of the request-target | Origin-server duty; client implication: with absolute-form the request-target authority wins over Host. |
| `9112 3-26` | MUST | A server must accept absolute-form in requests even from non-proxy clients. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST accept the absolute-form in requests | Server duty; client may safely rely on servers accepting absolute-form, though it should still default to origin-form for direct requests. |
| `9112 3-27` | informative | The authority-form request-target is used only for CONNECT requests. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The "authority-form" of request-target is only used for CONNECT requests | Emit authority-form if and only if the method is CONNECT; reject caller attempts to combine authority-form with other methods. |
| `9112 3-28` | informative | authority-form is uri-host ":" port — host and port with the colon mandatory. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | authority-form = uri-host ":" port | CONNECT target serializer must always include an explicit ":port"; a bare host is not valid authority-form. |
| `9112 3-29` | MUST | For CONNECT, the client must send only the host and port of the tunnel destination as the request-target. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a client MUST send only the host and port of the tunnel destination as the request-target | CONNECT request-target is host:port only — no scheme, no userinfo, no path, no query. |
| `9112 3-30` | informative | When the target URI elides the port, the client sends the scheme's default port in the CONNECT authority-form. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | it sends the scheme's default port if the target URI elides the port | Fill in the scheme default (80 for http, 443 for https) when building CONNECT host:port from a portless URI. |
| `9112 3-31` | informative | The asterisk-form request-target is used only for a server-wide OPTIONS request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The "asterisk-form" of request-target is only used for a server-wide OPTIONS request | Emit "*" only when method is OPTIONS and the request is server-wide; never for any other method. |
| `9112 3-32` | informative | asterisk-form is the single character "*". <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | asterisk-form = "*" | Server-wide OPTIONS request-target is exactly one octet %x2A, nothing appended. |
| `9112 3-33` | MUST | For a server-wide OPTIONS request, the client must send only "*" as the request-target. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client MUST send only "*" (%x2A) as the request-target | API for server-wide OPTIONS must serialize `OPTIONS * HTTP/1.1`; do not substitute "/" or the absolute URI. |
| `9112 3-34` | MUST | A last-hop proxy receiving OPTIONS in absolute-form with empty path and no query must forward it with request-target "*". <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | the last proxy on the request chain MUST send a request-target of "*" when it forwards the request to the indicated origin server | Proxy-only duty; client sending server-wide OPTIONS through a proxy uses absolute-form with empty path (e.g. `OPTIONS http://host:port HTTP/1.1`). |
| `9112 3-35` | informative | When the request-target is in absolute-form, it is itself the target URI. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The target URI is the request-target when the request-target is in absolute-form. | Server reconstruction rule; client implication: an absolute-form target fully determines the resource regardless of Host. |
| `9112 3-36` | informative | Absent a configured/gateway-provided scheme, the reconstructed target URI scheme is "https" on a secured connection, else "http". <br>_(server [not-client])_ | if the request is received over a secured connection, the target URI's scheme is "https"; if not, the scheme is "http" | Server-side algorithm; client just needs to pick TLS vs cleartext consistent with the URI scheme it intends. |
| `9112 3-37` | informative | If the request-target is authority-form, the reconstructed target URI's authority is the request-target. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the request-target is in authority-form, the target URI's authority component is the request-target. | Server reconstruction rule; underlines that the CONNECT host:port the client emits is taken as the authority verbatim. |
| `9112 3-38` | informative | Otherwise, the reconstructed target URI's authority is the Host header field value. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Otherwise, the target URI's authority component is the field value of the Host header field. | Server rule, but it is why the client's Host value (3-14) must exactly equal the intended authority — the server routes on it. |
| `9112 3-39` | informative | If Host is absent, empty, or invalid, the reconstructed authority component is empty. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If there is no Host header field or if its field value is empty or invalid, the target URI's authority component is empty. | Server rule; client that emits empty Host (3-15) must expect the server to see an empty authority and possibly reject. |
| `9112 3-40` | informative | If the request-target is authority-form or asterisk-form, the reconstructed combined path-and-query component is empty. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the request-target is in authority-form or asterisk-form, the target URI's combined path and query component is empty. | Server reconstruction rule; no client action beyond emitting the correct form. |
| `9112 3-41` | informative | Otherwise, the reconstructed combined path-and-query component is the request-target. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Otherwise, the target URI's combined path and query component is the request-target. | Server rule; the origin-form path?query the client emits becomes the resource path verbatim — preserve percent-encoding exactly. |
| `9112 3-42` | informative | The reconstructed components can be recombined into absolute-URI form as scheme + "://" + authority + path-and-query. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | can be recombined into absolute-URI form by concatenating the scheme, "://", authority, and combined path and query component | Server-side recomposition; useful as the reference algorithm if the client ever reconstructs effective URIs for redirects/logging. |
| `9112 3-43` | informative | When the authority is empty and the scheme requires one, an empty authority is replaced with the server's configured default before further processing. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | An empty authority is replaced with the configured default before further processing of the request. | Server configuration behavior; no client action. |
| `9112 3-44` | MAY | A server that can uniquely identify an authority from the request context may use that identity as the default authority. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that can uniquely identify an authority from the request context MAY use that identity as a default without this risk. | Server-only option; no client action. |

### RFC 9112 4-5 - Status Line and Field Syntax — HTTP/1.1 client conformance facts

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9112 4-5-1` | OPTIONAL | The first line of a response is the status-line: protocol version, single SP, status code, single SP, then an optional reason phrase. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | the status-line, consisting of the protocol version, a space (SP), the status code, and another space and ending with an OPTIONAL textual phrase | Response parser must split the first line as HTTP-version SP status-code SP [reason]; must handle both present and absent reason-phrase. |
| `9112 4-5-2` | informative | Grammar: status-line = HTTP-version SP status-code SP [ reason-phrase ] — reason-phrase bracketed as optional. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | status-line = HTTP-version SP status-code SP [ reason-phrase ] | Implement this ABNF in the response status-line parser; everything after the second SP up to the line terminator is the reason-phrase. |
| `9112 4-5-3` | MAY | A recipient may leniently parse the status-line on whitespace-delimited word boundaries, treating any whitespace form as the SP separator and ignoring leading/trailing whitespace. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | recipients MAY instead parse on whitespace-delimited word boundaries and, aside from the line terminator, treat any form of whitespace as the SP separator while ignoring preceding or trailing whitespace | Optional leniency: client parser may accept runs of SP/HTAB/VT/FF/bare-CR as element separators; if implemented, note the response-splitting risk the spec flags (§11.1) and pick one consistent policy. |
| `9112 4-5-4` | informative | The whitespace octets covered by the lenient status-line parsing allowance are exactly SP, HTAB, VT (%x0B), FF (%x0C), and bare CR. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | such whitespace includes one or more of the following octets: SP, HTAB, VT (%x0B), FF (%x0C), or bare CR | If lenient status-line parsing is implemented, the separator set is exactly SP, HTAB, VT %x0B, FF %x0C, bare CR — nothing else. |
| `9112 4-5-5` | informative | A recipient interprets the rest of the response per the semantics of a recognized status code, or per the code's class when the specific code is unrecognized. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | in light of the semantics defined for that status code, if the status code is recognized by that recipient, or in accordance with the class of that status code when the specific code is unrecognized | Client must not fail on unknown 3-digit codes: fall back to class semantics (first digit, treat as x00 of that class) for message framing and handling. |
| `9112 4-5-6` | informative | Grammar: the status code is exactly three digits. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | status-code = 3DIGIT | Parser must require exactly 3 ASCII digits for the status code; reject status-lines with fewer/more digits or non-digit characters. |
| `9112 4-5-7` | informative | Grammar: the reason phrase is one or more of HTAB, SP, VCHAR, or obs-text octets. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | reason-phrase = 1*( HTAB / SP / VCHAR / obs-text ) | Reason-phrase parser must tolerate HTAB, SP, all visible ASCII, and obs-text (0x80–0xFF) octets without erroring; bytes need not be valid UTF-8. |
| `9112 4-5-8` | SHOULD | A client should ignore the content of the reason phrase because it is not a reliable information channel. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client SHOULD ignore the reason-phrase content | Never base client logic (retry, error classification, etc.) on the reason phrase; at most retain it for logging/debugging. |
| `9112 4-5-9` | MUST | A server must send the SP separating status-code from reason-phrase even when the reason-phrase is absent, so the status-line ends with a space. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MUST send the space that separates the status-code from the reason-phrase even when the reason-phrase is absent | Client parser must accept 'HTTP/1.1 200 ' (trailing SP, empty reason-phrase) as valid; combined with 4-5-3 it may also tolerate the SP being absent. |
| `9112 4-5-10` | informative | Each field line is a case-insensitive field name, a colon, optional leading whitespace, the field line value, and optional trailing whitespace. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Each field line consists of a case-insensitive field name followed by a colon (":"), optional leading whitespace, the field line value, and optional trailing whitespace. | Field-name comparison and lookup must be case-insensitive; parse each header line as name ':' OWS value OWS. |
| `9112 4-5-11` | informative | Grammar: field-line = field-name ":" OWS field-value OWS. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | field-line = field-name ":" OWS field-value OWS | Implement this ABNF for both request serialization and response header parsing; OWS is *(SP / HTAB). |
| `9112 4-5-12` | informative | Field lines are parsed with a generic algorithm that does not depend on the individual field name. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Messages are parsed using a generic algorithm, independent of the individual field names. | The header-line tokenizer must be name-agnostic — no per-header syntax special-casing at the field-line parsing layer. |
| `9112 4-5-13` | informative | Field line value contents are not interpreted until a later stage, usually after the entire field section has been read. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The contents within a given field line value are not parsed until a later stage of message interpretation | Client should read the complete response field section before semantic interpretation of individual values (e.g., framing decisions after all headers are in). |
| `9112 4-5-14` | informative | No whitespace is permitted between the field name and the colon. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | No whitespace is allowed between the field name and colon. | Request serializer must never emit whitespace before ':'. For received responses the spec gives no client-side directive (only proxy MUST strip, 4-5-16); safest client policy is to reject or strip such whitespace — document the choice. |
| `9112 4-5-15` | MUST | A server must reject with 400 (Bad Request) any request containing whitespace between a header field name and colon. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST reject, with a response status code of 400 (Bad Request), any received request message that contains whitespace between a header field name and colon. | Server-only. Context for the client: emitting 'Name : value' will get requests rejected — reinforces 4-5-14 on the emit path. |
| `9112 4-5-16` | MUST | A proxy must remove whitespace between field name and colon from a response before forwarding it downstream. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MUST remove any such whitespace from a response message before forwarding the message downstream. | Proxy-only; a pure client has no forwarding obligation. Informs 4-5-14's note that responses may legitimately arrive already sanitized. |
| `9112 4-5-17` | informative | A single SP preceding the field line value is the preferred form of OWS when generating field lines. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a single SP preceding the field line value is preferred for consistent readability by humans | When serializing request headers, emit exactly 'Name: value' — one SP after the colon, no trailing OWS. |
| `9112 4-5-18` | informative | Parsers exclude leading and trailing OWS when extracting the field line value; the value does not include that whitespace. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | OWS occurring before the first non-whitespace octet of the field line value, or after the last non-whitespace octet of the field line value, is excluded by parsers | Response header parser must trim leading/trailing SP and HTAB from every field value before storing/exposing it. |
| `9112 4-5-19` | informative | Obsolete line folding (multi-line field values) is deprecated except within the message/http media type. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | This specification deprecates such line folding except within the "message/http" media type | Treat obs-fold as deprecated legacy: never produce it, and handle received folds only via the replacement rule (4-5-24). |
| `9112 4-5-20` | informative | Grammar: obs-fold = OWS CRLF RWS — a CRLF followed by at least one SP/HTAB continues the previous field line value. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | obs-fold = OWS CRLF RWS | Parser must recognize the pattern CRLF followed by SP/HTAB as obs-fold (a value continuation, not a new field line) so 4-5-24 can be applied. |
| `9112 4-5-21` | MUST NOT | A sender must not generate a message whose field line values contain line folding, unless it is packaged within message/http. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A sender MUST NOT generate a message that includes line folding (i.e., that has any field line value that contains a match to the obs-fold rule) | Request serializer must reject or refuse field values containing CR/LF sequences that would form obs-fold; never emit multi-line header values. |
| `9112 4-5-22` | MUST | A server receiving obs-fold in a request (outside message/http) must either reject with 400 or replace each obs-fold with one or more SP octets before interpreting/forwarding. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that receives an obs-fold in a request message that is not within a "message/http" container MUST either reject the message by sending a 400 (Bad Request) | Server-only; not applicable to the client's response-parsing path. |
| `9112 4-5-23` | MUST | A proxy or gateway receiving obs-fold in a response (outside message/http) must either discard it and send 502 (Bad Gateway) or replace each obs-fold with one or more SP octets before interpreting/forwarding. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | A proxy or gateway that receives an obs-fold in a response message that is not within a "message/http" container MUST either discard the message and replace it with a 502 (Bad Gateway) response | Proxy/gateway-only; the end-client rule is 4-5-24 (replace, no reject option). |
| `9112 4-5-24` | MUST | A user agent receiving obs-fold in a response (outside message/http) must replace each obs-fold with one or more SP octets before interpreting the field value. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | A user agent that receives an obs-fold in a response message that is not within a "message/http" container MUST replace each received obs-fold with one or more SP octets prior to interpreting the field value | Client response parser MUST unfold: on CRLF followed by SP/HTAB, splice the continuation into the current value with SP replacing the fold — the user-agent rule offers no reject alternative. Test with a folded header from the server. |

### RFC 9112 6 - Message Body: Transfer-Encoding, Content-Length, Message Body Length framing algorithm

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9112 6-1` | informative | The message body carries the content and is byte-identical to it unless a transfer coding has been applied. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The message body is identical to the content unless a transfer coding has been applied | Only run transfer-coding (e.g. chunked) removal on the body; content codings (Content-Encoding) are a separate layer left intact by the framing parser. |
| `9112 6-2` | informative | Grammar: a message body is any sequence of octets (message-body = *OCTET). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | message-body = *OCTET | Body reader must be 8-bit clean: accept any octet values including NUL and bytes that look like CRLF or headers. |
| `9112 6-3` | informative | A request has a body if and only if it carries a Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding header field; request framing is independent of method semantics. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The presence of a message body in a request is signaled by a Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding header field. | When emitting a request with a body, always include exactly one of Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding: chunked; a bodyless request carries neither. |
| `9112 6-4` | informative | Whether a response has a body depends on the request method it answers and the response status code, not only on its header fields. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The presence of a message body in a response, as detailed in Section 6.3, depends on both the request method to which it is responding and the response status code. | Response parser must receive the request method (esp. HEAD, CONNECT) as input to the body-length decision, not just the response headers. |
| `9112 6-5` | informative | Grammar: Transfer-Encoding is a comma-separated list of transfer-coding names (Transfer-Encoding = #transfer-coding). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Transfer-Encoding = #transfer-coding | Parse Transfer-Encoding with the #-list rules (comma-separated, OWS, possibly split across multiple field lines); the framing decision hinges on the FINAL element being 'chunked'. |
| `9112 6-6` | MUST | Every recipient must be able to parse the chunked transfer coding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST be able to parse the chunked transfer coding | Client must implement a full chunked decoder (chunk-size hex, extensions, last-chunk, trailer section, terminating CRLF) for response bodies. |
| `9112 6-7` | MUST NOT | A sender must not apply the chunked coding more than once to the same message body. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A sender MUST NOT apply the chunked transfer coding more than once to a message body | Emitter must never produce Transfer-Encoding with 'chunked' listed twice; reject/refuse to build such a request. |
| `9112 6-8` | MUST | If any non-chunked transfer coding is applied to a request's content, the sender must apply chunked as the final transfer coding. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If any transfer coding other than chunked is applied to a request's content, the sender MUST apply chunked as the final transfer coding | If the client ever emits gzip/other transfer codings on a request, it must append chunked last (e.g. 'Transfer-Encoding: gzip, chunked'); never send a request whose TE list does not end in chunked. |
| `9112 6-9` | MUST | If any non-chunked transfer coding is applied to a response's content, the server must either finish with chunked or close-delimit the response. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the sender MUST either apply chunked as the final transfer coding or terminate the message by closing the connection | Server-emit rule; the client-side consequence is parse rule 6-29: a response TE list not ending in chunked is read until connection close. |
| `9112 6-10` | MAY | Any recipient in the chain may decode received transfer codings or apply additional ones, provided it updates the Transfer-Encoding field value accordingly. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Any recipient along the request/response chain MAY decode the received transfer coding(s) or apply additional transfer coding(s) to the message body | Client may decode chunked before surfacing the body (typical); if it exposes Transfer-Encoding to callers after decoding, the field value must reflect the removal. |
| `9112 6-11` | MAY | Transfer-Encoding may appear on a response to HEAD or on a 304 response to GET, which carry no body, to indicate what coding would have been applied. <br>_(server,origin [CLIENT])_ | Transfer-Encoding MAY be sent in a response to a HEAD request or in a 304 (Not Modified) response | Client must NOT treat Transfer-Encoding on a HEAD or 304 response as implying a body; length rule 1 (6-23) wins and the message ends at the blank line. |
| `9112 6-12` | MUST NOT | A server must not send Transfer-Encoding in any 1xx or 204 response. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST NOT send a Transfer-Encoding header field in any response with a status code of 1xx (Informational) or 204 (No Content). | Server-only emit rule; robust client already ignores it there because rule 1 of the length algorithm terminates 1xx/204 at the blank line. |
| `9112 6-13` | MUST NOT | A server must not send Transfer-Encoding in any 2xx response to CONNECT. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST NOT send a Transfer-Encoding header field in any 2xx (Successful) response to a CONNECT request | Server-only emit rule; the client-side counterpart is 6-25 (MUST ignore CL/TE on 2xx-to-CONNECT). |
| `9112 6-14` | SHOULD | A server receiving a request with a transfer coding it does not understand should respond 501. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that receives a request message with a transfer coding it does not understand SHOULD respond with 501 (Not Implemented). | Server rule; client takeaway: only emit codings the server is known to support (in practice, only chunked) and be prepared for 501. |
| `9112 6-15` | MUST NOT | A client must not send Transfer-Encoding in a request unless it knows the server handles HTTP/1.1 or later. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT send a request containing Transfer-Encoding unless it knows the server will handle HTTP/1.1 requests (or later minor revisions) | Gate chunked/TE request emission on known server HTTP/1.1 support (configuration or a remembered prior response version); default to Content-Length otherwise. |
| `9112 6-16` | MUST NOT | A server must not send Transfer-Encoding in a response unless the corresponding request indicated HTTP/1.1 or later. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST NOT send a response containing Transfer-Encoding unless the corresponding request indicates HTTP/1.1 (or later minor revisions). | Server emit rule; an HTTP/1.1 client sending 1.1 requests may legitimately receive TE responses. |
| `9112 6-17` | MAY | A server may either reject a request bearing both Content-Length and Transfer-Encoding or process it per Transfer-Encoding alone. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY reject a request that contains both Content-Length and Transfer-Encoding or process such a request in accordance with the Transfer-Encoding alone. | Server option; client must never create the situation (6-21 forbids sending CL together with TE). |
| `9112 6-18` | MUST | After responding to a request that carried both Content-Length and Transfer-Encoding, the server must close the connection. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MUST close the connection after responding to such a request to avoid the potential attacks | Server rule (anti-smuggling); no client action beyond never sending CL+TE together. |
| `9112 6-19` | MUST | A server or client receiving an HTTP/1.0 message containing Transfer-Encoding must treat the framing as faulty and close the connection after processing the message. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | MUST treat the message as if the framing is faulty, even if a Content-Length is present, and close the connection after processing the message | If a response line says HTTP/1.0 and Transfer-Encoding is present, ignore any Content-Length, treat framing as faulty, and mark the connection not-reusable (close after processing). |
| `9112 6-20` | informative | Absent Transfer-Encoding, Content-Length gives the anticipated size of the content as a decimal number of octets; for bodyless messages it describes the selected representation instead. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a Content-Length header field (Section 8.6 of [HTTP]) can provide the anticipated size, as a decimal number of octets | Parse Content-Length strictly as decimal digits; on bodyless responses (HEAD/304) treat it as representation metadata, never as bytes to read. |
| `9112 6-21` | MUST NOT | A sender must not send Content-Length in any message that contains a Transfer-Encoding header field. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A sender MUST NOT send a Content-Length header field in any message that contains a Transfer-Encoding header field. | Request serializer must enforce mutual exclusion: if Transfer-Encoding is set, strip/refuse any Content-Length (and vice versa). |
| `9112 6-22` | informative | Message body length is determined by the 6.3 algorithm's rules applied strictly in order of precedence (rules 1 through 8). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The length of a message body is determined by one of the following (in order of precedence): | Implement body-length determination as an ordered cascade exactly matching rules 1-8; do not reorder checks (e.g. status-code rules must be evaluated before Transfer-Encoding/Content-Length). |
| `9112 6-23` | informative | Rule 1: any response to HEAD and any 1xx, 204, or 304 response ends at the first empty line after the headers and can carry no body or trailers, whatever header fields are present. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Any response to a HEAD request and any response with a 1xx (Informational), 204 (No Content), or 304 (Not Modified) status code is always terminated by the first empty line | Client parser: for HEAD responses and 1xx/204/304 statuses, read zero body octets regardless of Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding, then the connection is ready for the next response. |
| `9112 6-24` | informative | Rule 2: a 2xx response to CONNECT means the connection becomes a tunnel immediately after the empty line ending the header section. <br>_(client,proxy,tunnel [CLIENT])_ | Any 2xx (Successful) response to a CONNECT request implies that the connection will become a tunnel immediately after the empty line that concludes the header fields. | On 2xx-to-CONNECT, stop HTTP parsing at the blank line and hand the raw connection over as an opaque tunnel; bytes after the blank line belong to the tunnel. |
| `9112 6-25` | MUST | Rule 2: a client must ignore any Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding fields received in a 2xx response to CONNECT. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST ignore any Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding header fields received in such a message. | In the 2xx-to-CONNECT path, discard CL/TE from the framing decision entirely (do not read a body, do not error); tunnel starts right after the header section. |
| `9112 6-26` | informative | Rule 3: when a received message has both Transfer-Encoding and Content-Length, Transfer-Encoding overrides Content-Length; such a message may indicate smuggling/splitting and ought to be handled as an error. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If a message is received with both a Transfer-Encoding and a Content-Length header field, the Transfer-Encoding overrides the Content-Length. | Client receiving a response with both fields must frame by Transfer-Encoding and ignore Content-Length; treating it as an error (reject + close) is the safer permitted choice. |
| `9112 6-27` | MUST | Rule 3: an intermediary that forwards a message with both fields must first remove the received Content-Length and process the Transfer-Encoding before forwarding. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | An intermediary that chooses to forward the message MUST first remove the received Content-Length field and process the Transfer-Encoding (as described below) prior to forwarding the message downstream. | Intermediary-only; not applicable to a pure client library. |
| `9112 6-28` | informative | Rule 4: if Transfer-Encoding is present and chunked is the final encoding, body length is determined by decoding the chunked data until the coding signals completion. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | the message body length is determined by reading and decoding the chunked data until the transfer coding indicates the data is complete | When the response TE list ends in chunked, drive the chunked decoder to the last-chunk + trailer + final CRLF; that, not connection state, defines end-of-message. |
| `9112 6-29` | informative | Rule 4: if Transfer-Encoding is present in a response and chunked is not the final encoding, body length is determined by reading until the server closes the connection. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the message body length is determined by reading the connection until it is closed by the server | Response with TE whose final coding is not chunked: read to EOF, deliver everything as body, and mark the connection dead (no reuse). |
| `9112 6-30` | MUST | Rule 4: if Transfer-Encoding is present in a request and chunked is not the final encoding, the length is unreliable and the server must respond 400 and close the connection. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MUST respond with the 400 (Bad Request) status code and then close the connection | Server rule; the client-side guard is 6-8 — never emit a request whose TE list does not end in chunked, or expect a 400 + close. |
| `9112 6-31` | MUST | Rule 5: without Transfer-Encoding, an invalid Content-Length makes the framing invalid and the recipient must treat it as an unrecoverable error — unless the value parses as a comma-separated list whose members are all valid and all identical, in which case that single value is used. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | the message framing is invalid and the recipient MUST treat it as an unrecoverable error, unless the field value can be successfully parsed as a comma-separated list | Content-Length validator: strict decimal parse; on failure, try comma-list — accept only if every element is a valid identical value (also covers repeated CL field lines); anything else is an unrecoverable framing error. |
| `9112 6-32` | MUST | Rule 5: if the unrecoverable Content-Length error is in a request, the server must respond 400 and close the connection. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the unrecoverable error is in a request message, the server MUST respond with a 400 (Bad Request) status code and then close the connection. | Server-only branch of rule 5. |
| `9112 6-33` | MUST | Rule 5: if the unrecoverable error is in a response received by a proxy, the proxy must close the upstream connection, discard the response, and send 502 to its client. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | the proxy MUST close the connection to the server, discard the received response, and send a 502 (Bad Gateway) response to the client | Proxy-only branch of rule 5. |
| `9112 6-34` | MUST | Rule 5: if the unrecoverable error is in a response received by a user agent, it must close the connection to the server and discard the received response. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | the user agent MUST close the connection to the server and discard the received response | On invalid Content-Length in a response (not saved by the identical-list exception): surface an error to the caller, deliver no body, and close/evict the connection — never guess a length. |
| `9112 6-35` | informative | Rule 6: a valid Content-Length without Transfer-Encoding defines the expected body length in octets via its decimal value. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If a valid Content-Length header field is present without Transfer-Encoding, its decimal value defines the expected message body length in octets. | Read exactly N body octets after the header section; bytes beyond N belong to the next message on the connection. |
| `9112 6-36` | MUST | Rule 6: if the sender closes or the recipient times out before the declared number of octets arrive, the recipient must consider the message incomplete and close the connection. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the sender closes the connection or the recipient times out before the indicated number of octets are received, the recipient MUST consider the message to be incomplete and close the connection. | EOF or timeout with fewer than Content-Length octets read → report a truncated/incomplete-body error (not a successful short body) and close; never return the partial body as complete. |
| `9112 6-37` | informative | Rule 7: a request matching none of the earlier rules has a body length of zero (no body). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If this is a request message and none of the above are true, then the message body length is zero (no message body is present). | Emit-side corollary for the client: omitting both Content-Length and Transfer-Encoding declares a zero-length request body — safe for GET/HEAD-style requests. |
| `9112 6-38` | informative | Rule 8: otherwise a response has no declared body length and the body is whatever octets arrive before the server closes the connection. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | this is a response message without a declared message body length, so the message body length is determined by the number of octets received prior to the server closing the connection | Client must support close-delimited responses: no CL/TE on an eligible status → read to EOF as the body and mark the connection non-reusable. |
| `9112 6-39` | SHOULD | Because a completed close-delimited response is indistinguishable from a truncated one, a server should generate encoding- or length-delimited messages whenever possible. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | a server SHOULD generate encoding or length-delimited messages whenever possible | Server rule; client note: a close-delimited body can never be verified complete — do not treat EOF-delimited bodies as integrity-checked. |
| `9112 6-40` | informative | Request messages are never close-delimited: they are always framed by length or transfer coding, and the absence of both means the request ends right after the header section. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Request messages are never close-delimited because they are always explicitly framed by length or transfer coding | Client must never rely on half-closing the connection to delimit a request body; every request body needs Content-Length or chunked. |
| `9112 6-41` | MAY | A server may reject a request that has a body but no Content-Length with 411 (Length Required). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY reject a request that contains a message body but not a Content-Length by responding with 411 (Length Required). | Server option; motivates client fact 6-42 (prefer Content-Length) and means the client should expect possible 411 when sending chunked. |
| `9112 6-42` | SHOULD | Unless a non-chunked transfer coding was applied, a client sending a request body should use a valid Content-Length when the length is known in advance, rather than chunked. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a client that sends a request containing a message body SHOULD use a valid Content-Length header field if the message body length is known in advance | Request builder default: known-length body → Content-Length; use chunked only for unknown-length/streamed bodies. |
| `9112 6-43` | MUST | A user agent sending a request with a body must include either a valid Content-Length or use the chunked transfer coding. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | A user agent that sends a request that contains a message body MUST send either a valid Content-Length header field or use the chunked transfer coding. | Serializer invariant: any non-empty request body implies exactly one of a valid Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding ending in chunked; refuse to send a bodied request with neither. |
| `9112 6-44` | MUST NOT | A client must not use the chunked transfer coding unless it knows the server handles HTTP/1.1 (or later) requests. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT use the chunked transfer coding unless it knows the server will handle HTTP/1.1 (or later) requests | Gate chunked request bodies on known server HTTP/1.1 support (configuration or remembered response version); otherwise buffer to compute Content-Length. |
| `9112 6-45` | MAY | If extra data remains after the final response on a connection is completely received, a user agent may discard it or try to attribute it to the prior message body (e.g. wrong Content-Length). <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | a user agent MAY discard the remaining data or attempt to determine if that data belongs as part of the prior message body | On trailing garbage after the last framed response, discarding it (and closing the connection) is the simple conforming choice. |
| `9112 6-46` | MUST NOT | A client must not process, cache, or forward extra data left after a completed response as if it were a separate response (cache-poisoning risk). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT process, cache, or forward such extra data as a separate response | Never parse leftover bytes beyond the last expected response as a new response; there must be an outstanding request for a response to be attributed to — otherwise discard and close. |

### RFC 9112 7 - Transfer Codings

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9112 7-1` | informative | Transfer-coding names are case-insensitive. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | All transfer-coding names are case-insensitive | Compare coding names in Transfer-Encoding/TE case-insensitively; "Chunked" and "CHUNKED" must match "chunked". |
| `9112 7-2` | informative | The chunked body consists of zero or more chunks, a last-chunk, a trailer section, and a final CRLF. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | chunked-body = *chunk last-chunk trailer-section CRLF | Chunked decoder state machine: chunks* -> last-chunk -> trailer-section -> terminating CRLF; body incomplete until final empty line consumed. |
| `9112 7-3` | informative | Each chunk is a chunk-size, optional chunk extensions, CRLF, chunk-data, CRLF. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | chunk = chunk-size [ chunk-ext ] CRLF chunk-data CRLF | Per chunk: parse hex size, optionally skip extensions, require CRLF, read exactly size octets, require trailing CRLF (error if absent). |
| `9112 7-4` | informative | chunk-size is one or more hexadecimal digits giving the size of chunk-data in octets. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | chunk-size = 1*HEXDIG | Parse 1+ hex digits (accept both cases per HEXDIG); empty chunk-size is a parse error; value is the exact chunk-data octet count. |
| `9112 7-5` | informative | The last chunk is one or more "0" digits, optional extensions, and CRLF. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | last-chunk = 1*("0") [ chunk-ext ] CRLF | Treat any all-zeros chunk-size ("0", "00", "000") as last-chunk; extensions may still follow the zeros before CRLF. |
| `9112 7-6` | informative | chunk-data is exactly chunk-size opaque octets. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | chunk-data = 1*OCTET ; a sequence of chunk-size octets | Read exactly chunk-size raw octets without interpretation; do not scan for CRLF inside chunk-data. |
| `9112 7-7` | informative | Chunked coding completes on a zero-size chunk, possibly followed by a trailer section, terminated by an empty line. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The chunked transfer coding is complete when a chunk with a chunk-size of zero is received, possibly followed by a trailer section, and finally terminated by an empty line. | Do not signal end-of-body at the zero chunk alone; keep reading trailer field-lines until the empty line, then the message is complete. |
| `9112 7-8` | MUST | A recipient must be able to parse and decode chunked transfer coding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST be able to parse and decode the chunked transfer coding. | Chunked response decoding is mandatory in the client; it cannot be feature-flagged off or rejected. |
| `9112 7-9` | MUST | Recipients must guard chunk-size parsing against integer overflow and precision loss from large hexadecimal numerals. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | recipients MUST anticipate potentially large hexadecimal numerals and prevent parsing errors due to integer conversion overflows or precision loss due to integer representation | Bound chunk-size parse: detect uint64 overflow (or cap accepted hex digits) and fail the message cleanly instead of wrapping/truncating. |
| `9112 7-10` | SHOULD | The chunked coding defines no parameters; their presence should be treated as an error. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The chunked coding does not define any parameters. Their presence SHOULD be treated as an error. | Reject Transfer-Encoding values like "chunked;foo=bar" (parameters on the coding name, distinct from per-chunk chunk-ext). |
| `9112 7-11` | informative | Chunk extensions are zero or more ";"-separated name[=value] pairs after chunk-size, with optional bad whitespace around ";" and "=". <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | chunk-ext = *( BWS ";" BWS chunk-ext-name [ BWS "=" BWS chunk-ext-val ] ) | Extension parser must tolerate optional whitespace (BWS) around ";" and "=" and accept extensions with or without values. |
| `9112 7-12` | informative | Chunk extension names are tokens; values are token or quoted-string. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | chunk-ext-name = token chunk-ext-val = token / quoted-string | To skip extensions safely the parser needs quoted-string handling: a ";" or CR inside a quoted ext value must not terminate the extension. |
| `9112 7-13` | MUST | A recipient must ignore unrecognized chunk extensions. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST ignore unrecognized chunk extensions. | Parse-and-discard unknown chunk extensions without failing the message; ignoring is mandatory, not optional. |
| `9112 7-14` | informative | A server ought to limit total chunk-extension length in requests and respond 4xx when exceeded. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server ought to limit the total length of chunk extensions received in a request to an amount reasonable for the services provided | Server-side guidance; a robust client may mirror it with a defensive bound on total extension bytes skipped per message (peer-input policy). |
| `9112 7-15` | informative | The trailer section is zero or more field-lines each terminated by CRLF. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | trailer-section = *( field-line CRLF ) | Parse trailers with the same field-line grammar as headers, one per CRLF-terminated line, ended by the empty line. |
| `9112 7-16` | MAY | A recipient removing chunked coding may selectively retain or discard received trailer fields. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient that removes the chunked coding from a message MAY selectively retain or discard the received trailer fields. | Client may drop trailers or surface them; either policy is conformant — pick one and document it. |
| `9112 7-17` | MUST | A recipient retaining a trailer field must either store/forward it separately from header fields or merge it into the header section. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient that retains a received trailer field MUST either store/forward the trailer field separately from the received header fields or merge the received trailer field into the header section. | If trailers are exposed in the response API, keep them in a distinct trailers collection, not silently appended to the header list. |
| `9112 7-18` | MUST NOT | A recipient must not merge a trailer field into the header section unless the field's definition explicitly permits and instructs safe merging. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST NOT merge a received trailer field into the header section unless its corresponding header field definition explicitly permits and instructs how the trailer field value can be safely merged. | Never auto-merge trailers into response headers; absent per-field merge knowledge, always keep them separate (satisfies 7-17 too). |
| `9112 7-19` | informative | Chunked decoding loops reading chunk-size/ext/CRLF, appending chunk-data and accumulating length while chunk-size > 0, then reads trailer fields until empty. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | while (chunk-size > 0) { read chunk-data and CRLF append chunk-data to content length := length + chunk-size read chunk-size, chunk-ext (if any), and CRLF } | Decoder loop must track cumulative decoded length and re-parse size+ext+CRLF per chunk; trailer loop runs after the zero chunk. |
| `9112 7-20` | informative | After decoding, the message's Content-Length becomes the decoded length and "chunked" is removed from Transfer-Encoding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Length := length Remove "chunked" from Transfer-Encoding | Once de-chunked, report body length as the decoded octet count and do not present "chunked" in any surfaced Transfer-Encoding metadata. |
| `9112 7-21` | informative | The compression transfer codings compress (x-compress), deflate, and gzip (x-gzip) use the same algorithms as the corresponding content codings. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The following transfer coding names for compression are defined by the same algorithm as their corresponding content coding: | If the client advertises gzip/deflate/compress in TE, decode with the content-coding algorithms (HTTP §8.4.1) and treat x-gzip/x-compress as aliases. |
| `9112 7-22` | SHOULD | Parameters on the compression codings should be treated as an error. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The presence of parameters with any of these compression codings SHOULD be treated as an error. | Reject transfer-coding values like "gzip;q=x" appearing in Transfer-Encoding (q in TE is a pseudo-parameter, not a coding parameter). |
| `9112 7-23` | MUST | Transfer coding registrations must include Name, Description, and a pointer to specification text. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Registrations MUST include the following fields: | IANA registry policy; nothing to implement in client code. |
| `9112 7-24` | MUST NOT | Transfer coding names must not overlap content coding names unless the transformation is identical. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Names of transfer codings MUST NOT overlap with names of content codings (Section 8.4.1 of [HTTP]) unless the encoding transformation is identical | Registry policy; client may safely share one decoder table for gzip/deflate/compress across transfer and content coding roles. |
| `9112 7-25` | SHOULD NOT | Future transfer coding registrations should not define parameters named "q". <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Future registrations of transfer codings SHOULD NOT define parameters called "q" (case-insensitively) in order to avoid ambiguities. | Registry policy; justifies the client parsing "q" in TE exclusively as a rank pseudo-parameter. |
| `9112 7-26` | MUST | New registry values require IETF Review and must conform to the purpose of transfer coding. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Values to be added to this namespace require IETF Review (see Section 4.8 of [RFC8126]) and MUST conform to the purpose of transfer coding defined in this specification. | IANA registry policy; nothing to implement in client code. |
| `9112 7-27` | informative | Using program names to identify encoding formats is discouraged for future encodings. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Use of program names for the identification of encoding formats is not desirable and is discouraged for future encodings. | Registry guidance only; x-compress/x-gzip legacy aliases remain (see 7-21). |
| `9112 7-28` | informative | TE indicates which non-chunked transfer codings the client accepts in the response and whether it preserves trailer fields. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | is used in HTTP/1.1 to indicate what transfer codings, besides chunked, the client is willing to accept in the response and whether the client is willing to preserve trailer fields | Only list codings in TE that the client can actually decode; include "trailers" only if trailers are surfaced (see 7-34). |
| `9112 7-29` | MUST NOT | A client must not send "chunked" in TE; chunked is always acceptable to HTTP/1.1 recipients. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT send the chunked transfer coding name in TE; chunked is always acceptable for HTTP/1.1 recipients. | TE emitter must filter/refuse "chunked" as a member; validate user-supplied TE values against this. |
| `9112 7-30` | MAY | The client may rank multiple acceptable transfer codings in TE with a case-insensitive "q" parameter. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client MAY rank the codings by preference using a case-insensitive "q" parameter | Optional: support emitting q ranks in TE; "q" must be matched case-insensitively ("Q=0.5" is valid). |
| `9112 7-31` | informative | The q rank value is a real number 0 through 1; 0.001 is least preferred, 1 most preferred, and 0 means not acceptable. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The rank value is a real number in the range 0 through 1, where 0.001 is the least preferred and 1 is the most preferred; a value of 0 means "not acceptable" | If emitting q values, constrain them to [0,1] with 0 meaning "not acceptable"; validate any caller-provided ranks. |
| `9112 7-32` | informative | If TE is empty or absent, chunked is the only acceptable transfer coding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the TE field value is empty or if no TE field is present, the only acceptable transfer coding is chunked. | A client not sending TE must still fully support chunked responses (with 7-8); receiving any other transfer coding unsolicited is a peer violation the client may reject. |
| `9112 7-33` | informative | A message with no transfer coding is always acceptable. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A message with no transfer coding is always acceptable. | Client must accept un-encoded responses (Content-Length or EOF delimited) regardless of what TE advertised. |
| `9112 7-34` | informative | The "trailers" keyword in TE means the sender will not discard trailer fields. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The keyword "trailers" indicates that the sender will not discard trailer fields | Send "TE: trailers" only if the client actually preserves and surfaces trailer fields (ties to 7-16/7-17 policy). |
| `9112 7-35` | MUST | A sender of TE must also send a "TE" connection option in the Connection header field. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a sender of TE MUST also send a "TE" connection option within the Connection header field (Section 7.6.1 of [HTTP]) in order to prevent the TE header field from being forwarded | Whenever the request includes a TE header, automatically add "TE" to the Connection header (emit "Connection: TE" or append to existing list). |

### RFC 9112 8-9 - Handling Incomplete Messages; Connection Management (persistence, retrying, pipelining, concurrency, failures/timeouts, tear-down, TLS)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9112 8-9-1` | MAY | A server receiving an incomplete request may send an error response before closing the connection. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that receives an incomplete request message, usually due to a canceled request or a triggered timeout exception, MAY send an error response prior to closing the connection. | No client action; be prepared that an aborted upload may still yield a readable error response before close. |
| `9112 8-9-2` | MUST | A client receiving an incomplete response (premature close or failed chunked decoding) must record the message as incomplete. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client that receives an incomplete response message, which can occur when a connection is closed prematurely or when decoding a supposedly chunked transfer coding fails, MUST record the message as incomplete. | Response object/error path must carry an explicit incomplete flag when body ends early or chunked decode fails; never silently return truncated body as complete. |
| `9112 8-9-3` | informative | If a response terminates mid-header-section, the client cannot assume the response's meaning was conveyed and may need to repeat the request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client cannot assume that meaning has been conveyed; the client might need to repeat the request in order to determine what action to take next. | Treat EOF before the empty line ending the header section as a hard incomplete-response error, not a usable status; surface it so retry logic can decide. |
| `9112 8-9-4` | informative | A chunked message body is incomplete unless the zero-sized terminating chunk has been received. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A message body that uses the chunked transfer coding is incomplete if the zero-sized chunk that terminates the encoding has not been received. | Chunked decoder must only mark body complete after the last-chunk (size 0) plus trailer/final CRLF; EOF before that = incomplete. |
| `9112 8-9-5` | informative | A message with a valid Content-Length is incomplete if fewer body octets than the declared value were received. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A message that uses a valid Content-Length is incomplete if the size of the message body received (in octets) is less than the value given by Content-Length. | Count received body octets against Content-Length; connection close before reaching the count = incomplete response error. |
| `9112 8-9-6` | informative | A response with neither chunked coding nor Content-Length is delimited by connection close and is complete if the header section arrived intact, unless the connection itself signaled an error. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | is terminated by closure of the connection and, if the header section was received intact, is considered complete unless an error was indicated by the underlying connection | For read-until-close bodies: clean close (e.g., TLS close_notify received) = complete; connection error/incomplete TLS close = incomplete (see 8-9-69). |
| `9112 8-9-7` | informative | HTTP presumes only a reliable transport with in-order delivery of requests and responses. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | HTTP only presumes a reliable transport with in-order delivery of requests and the corresponding in-order delivery of responses. | Run HTTP/1.1 only over reliable ordered byte streams (TCP/TLS); no datagram transports. |
| `9112 8-9-8` | informative | Connection protocol/port are determined by client configuration and target URI; "http" defaults to TCP port 80. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the "http" URI scheme (Section 4.2.1 of [HTTP]) indicates a default connection of TCP over IP, with a default TCP port of 80 | Dialer must default to port 80 for http (443 for https) when the URI has no explicit port, and honor proxy configuration overrides. |
| `9112 8-9-9` | informative | Each HTTP connection maps to exactly one underlying transport connection. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Each HTTP connection maps to one underlying transport connection. | Pool bookkeeping: one HTTP/1.1 connection object per TCP/TLS connection; no multiplexing of two HTTP connections over one socket. |
