| `9110 11-28` | MAY | A server may send WWW-Authenticate in responses other than 401 to indicate that supplying (different) credentials might affect the response. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY generate a WWW-Authenticate header field in other response messages | Client parser must accept WWW-Authenticate on any status code without error; optionally expose it to callers on non-401 responses. |
| `9110 11-29` | MUST NOT | A proxy forwarding a response must not modify any WWW-Authenticate header fields in it. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy forwarding a response MUST NOT modify any WWW-Authenticate header fields in that response. | Proxy-only; irrelevant to a pure client, but if the library ever forwards responses it must pass WWW-Authenticate through byte-for-byte. |
| `9110 11-30` | informative | User agents must parse WWW-Authenticate carefully: it can hold multiple challenges, each with its own comma-separated parameter list, in one field value. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | User agents are advised to take special care in parsing the field value, as it might contain more than one challenge | Implement a real challenge-list parser: commas separate both auth-params and challenges; a new challenge starts where a token is followed by SP-or-comma rather than "=". Test with the RFC's Basic+Newauth example. |
| `9110 11-31` | informative | The WWW-Authenticate header field itself can occur multiple times in one response. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | Furthermore, the header field itself can occur multiple times. | Collect challenges across all WWW-Authenticate field lines, not just the first occurrence. |
| `9110 11-32` | informative | Sending more than one challenge on the same WWW-Authenticate field line might not be interoperable with some user agents. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | sending a WWW-Authenticate field value with more than one member on the same field line might not be interoperable | Sender-side warning, but the client parser must be one of the agents that DOES handle multi-member field lines correctly. |
| `9110 11-33` | informative | Because challenge uses list syntax, a comma-whitespace-comma sequence is ambiguous (trailing list vs empty entry) but semantically harmless; parsers must tolerate empty list elements. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | a sequence of comma, whitespace, and comma can be considered either as applying to the preceding challenge, or to be an empty entry in the list of challenges | Skip empty elements between commas when splitting the challenge list; do not treat ", ," as a parse error. |
| `9110 11-34` | informative | Grammar: the Authorization field value is a single credentials production. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Authorization = credentials | Emit exactly one credentials value in Authorization; it is not a list — never send two Authorization values for one request. |
| `9110 11-35` | informative | If a request is authenticated and a realm specified, the same credentials are presumed valid for all other requests within that realm (unless the scheme requires otherwise). <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | If a request is authenticated and a realm specified, the same credentials are presumed to be valid for all other requests within this realm | Credential cache may auto-apply per realm, but must allow scheme-specific opt-out (e.g. challenge-dependent or clock-synchronized credentials). |
| `9110 11-36` | MUST NOT | A proxy forwarding a request must not modify any Authorization header fields in it. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy forwarding a request MUST NOT modify any Authorization header fields in that request. | Proxy/cache-only (see RFC 9111 §3.5 for cache handling); no client action beyond emitting the field correctly. |
| `9110 11-37` | informative | Grammar: Authentication-Info is a #-list of auth-params. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Authentication-Info = #auth-param | Parse Authentication-Info with the generic auth-param list parser (names case-insensitive, token or quoted-string values). |
| `9110 11-38` | informative | Authentication-Info can appear in any HTTP response, regardless of request method or status code. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The Authentication-Info field can be used in any HTTP response, independently of request method and status code. | Do not restrict Authentication-Info handling to 2xx or specific methods; accept it wherever it appears. |
| `9110 11-39` | informative | The semantics of Authentication-Info are defined by the auth scheme indicated by the Authorization header of the corresponding request. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | Its semantics are defined by the authentication scheme indicated by the Authorization header field (Section 11.6.2) of the corresponding request. | Interpret Authentication-Info params per the scheme the client sent in Authorization; if none was sent, the field carries no defined meaning. |
| `9110 11-40` | informative | A proxy forwarding a response is not allowed to modify the Authentication-Info field value in any way. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy forwarding a response is not allowed to modify the field value in any way. | Proxy-only pass-through rule; no client action. |
| `9110 11-41` | informative | Authentication-Info can be sent as a trailer field when the authentication scheme explicitly allows this. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | Authentication-Info can be sent as a trailer field (Section 6.5) when the authentication scheme explicitly allows this. | If the client surfaces trailers, include Authentication-Info from trailers when the scheme in use permits it; do not assume header-section-only. |
| `9110 11-42` | MUST | A proxy must send at least one Proxy-Authenticate header field in every 407 response it generates. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MUST send at least one Proxy-Authenticate header field in each 407 (Proxy Authentication Required) response that it generates. | Proxy duty; client may rely on its presence to drive the proxy-auth retry but should fail gracefully if a broken 407 lacks it. |
| `9110 11-43` | informative | Grammar: Proxy-Authenticate is a #-list of one or more challenges. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Proxy-Authenticate = #challenge | Reuse the WWW-Authenticate challenge-list parser for Proxy-Authenticate (multiple challenges, multiple field lines). |
| `9110 11-44` | informative | Proxy-Authenticate applies only to the next outbound client on the response chain, not to downstream recipients. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | the Proxy-Authenticate header field applies only to the next outbound client on the response chain | Treat a received Proxy-Authenticate as addressed to this client (which chose the proxy); answer it with Proxy-Authorization on the retried request, not Authorization. |
| `9110 11-45` | informative | The WWW-Authenticate parsing considerations (multiple challenges, comma lists, multiple field lines, empty entries) apply equally to Proxy-Authenticate. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the parsing considerations for WWW-Authenticate apply to this header field as well | Run the same multi-challenge/multi-line/empty-element test matrix against Proxy-Authenticate parsing. |
| `9110 11-46` | informative | Grammar: the Proxy-Authorization field value is a single credentials production. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Proxy-Authorization = credentials | Emit exactly one credentials value in Proxy-Authorization, using the same serializer as Authorization. |
| `9110 11-47` | informative | Proxy-Authorization applies only to the next inbound proxy that demanded authentication via Proxy-Authenticate; it is consumed by the first expecting proxy. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | the Proxy-Authorization header field applies only to the next inbound proxy that demanded authentication using the Proxy-Authenticate header field | Send Proxy-Authorization only on requests routed through the challenging proxy; never copy it onto direct-origin requests or a different proxy. |
| `9110 11-48` | MAY | A proxy may relay the client's credentials to the next proxy when proxies cooperatively authenticate a request. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MAY relay the credentials from the client request to the next proxy if that is the mechanism by which the proxies cooperatively authenticate a given request. | Proxy-chain behavior; no client action, though the client may observe one set of proxy credentials satisfying a proxy hierarchy. |
| `9110 11-49` | informative | Proxy-Authentication-Info is equivalent to Authentication-Info except it applies to proxy authentication and its semantics come from the scheme in the request's Proxy-Authorization. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | is equivalent to Authentication-Info, except that it applies to proxy authentication | Interpret Proxy-Authentication-Info params per the scheme the client sent in Proxy-Authorization; keep it separate from origin-auth state. |
| `9110 11-50` | informative | Grammar: Proxy-Authentication-Info is a #-list of auth-params. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Proxy-Authentication-Info = #auth-param | Reuse the generic auth-param list parser for Proxy-Authentication-Info. |
| `9110 11-51` | informative | Proxy-Authentication-Info applies only to the next outbound client on the response chain. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | the Proxy-Authentication-Info header field applies only to the next outbound client on the response chain | Treat a received Proxy-Authentication-Info as addressed to this client from the proxy it authenticated to; do not associate it with origin-server auth. |
| `9110 11-52` | informative | Proxy-Authentication-Info can be sent as a trailer field when the authentication scheme explicitly allows this. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | Proxy-Authentication-Info can be sent as a trailer field (Section 6.5) when the authentication scheme explicitly allows this. | If trailers are surfaced, include Proxy-Authentication-Info from trailers when the scheme in use permits it. |

### RFC 9110 12 - Content Negotiation

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9110 12-1` | MAY | A user agent is permitted (not required) to send request header fields describing its content preferences to aid proactive negotiation. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | In order to improve the server's guess, a user agent MAY send request header fields that describe its preferences. | Expose optional API for caller to set Accept/Accept-Charset/Accept-Encoding/Accept-Language; never mandatory. |
| `9110 12-2` | informative | A user agent must not assume the server will honor its proactive negotiation preferences. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | A user agent cannot rely on proactive negotiation preferences being consistently honored | Always inspect actual Content-Type/Content-Encoding/Content-Language of the response; never assume it matches the sent Accept* fields. |
| `9110 12-3` | informative | Accept* preferences sent in a request apply to all response content, including error and status representations, not just the target resource. <br>_(user-agent,server [CLIENT])_ | The preferences sent in these fields apply to any content in the response, including representations of the target resource, representations of error or processing status | Apply the same body decoding path (e.g., gzip) to 4xx/5xx bodies as to 2xx bodies. |
| `9110 12-4` | informative | 300 and 406 responses carry information about available alternative representations for reactive negotiation by the user agent. <br>_(server,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the alternatives listed in responses with the 300 (Multiple Choices) and 406 (Not Acceptable) status codes include information about available representations | Surface 300/406 status, headers, and body to the caller intact (they are data, not transport errors) so the caller can re-request an alternative. |
| `9110 12-5` | informative | Servers may send Accept and Accept-Encoding in responses to state preferred media types and content codings for subsequent requests to that resource (request content negotiation). <br>_(server,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the Accept (Section 12.5.1) and Accept-Encoding (Section 12.5.3) header fields can be sent in a response to indicate preferred media types and content codings for subsequent requests | Do not reject or drop Accept/Accept-Encoding appearing in a response; expose them so callers can shape subsequent request content. |
| `9110 12-6` | informative | Absence of a content negotiation field in a request means the sender has no preference on that dimension. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a request that does not contain the field implies that the sender has no preference on that dimension of negotiation | When the caller sets no negotiation header, omit it entirely; understand omission signals 'anything acceptable' (see 12-32 for Accept-Encoding consequence). |
| `9110 12-7` | informative | When no available representation is acceptable per a negotiation field, the origin server may either send 406 or ignore the field. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server can either honor the header field by sending a 406 (Not Acceptable) response or disregard the header field | Client must handle both outcomes after sending Accept* fields: a 406 response, or a representation that ignores its stated preferences. |
| `9110 12-8` | informative | The weight parameter name "q" is matched case-insensitively. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | use a common parameter, named "q" (case-insensitive), to assign a relative "weight" to the preference for that associated kind of content | When parsing negotiation fields (e.g., Accept-Encoding in responses), match parameter name q/Q case-insensitively as weight. |
| `9110 12-9` | informative | Quality values are real numbers in [0,1]; 0.001 is least preferred, 1 most preferred, and 0 means the value is not acceptable. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The weight is normalized to a real number in the range 0 through 1, where 0.001 is the least preferred and 1 is the most preferred; a value of 0 means "not acceptable" | Validate/clamp caller-supplied weights to [0,1]; treat q=0 as exclusion when constructing or evaluating negotiation fields. |
| `9110 12-10` | informative | A negotiation list member without a q parameter has default weight 1. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If no "q" parameter is present, the default weight is 1. | When evaluating negotiation fields, assign weight 1 to members lacking q; when emitting, omitting q must be equivalent to q=1. |
| `9110 12-11` | informative | Grammar: weight is OWS ";" OWS "q=" qvalue; qvalue is 0 with up to three decimal digits, or 1 with up to three zeros. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | weight = OWS ";" OWS "q=" qvalue qvalue = ( "0" [ "." 0*3DIGIT ] ) / ( "1" [ "." 0*3("0") ] ) | Serialize weights exactly as ;q= followed by a qvalue matching this ABNF (0, 0.d, 0.dd, 0.ddd, 1, 1.0, 1.00, 1.000); parser must accept OWS around the semicolon. |
| `9110 12-12` | MUST NOT | A sender must not generate a qvalue with more than three digits after the decimal point. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A sender of qvalue MUST NOT generate more than three digits after the decimal point. | Round/truncate caller-supplied weights to at most three decimal places before serializing any q parameter. |
| `9110 12-13` | informative | Where a field defines a wildcard "*", it selects unspecified values; if no wildcard is present, values not explicitly listed are unacceptable. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If no wildcard is present, values that are not explicitly mentioned in the field are considered unacceptable. | When building Accept/Accept-Charset/Accept-Encoding, include "*" (or "*/*") if the caller wants unlisted values to remain acceptable; otherwise the list is exhaustive. |
| `9110 12-14` | informative | In Vary, the wildcard "*" means the variance is unlimited. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Within Vary, the wildcard value means that the variance is unlimited. | Parse Vary: * as 'response varies on unbounded request aspects' — distinct from a field-name list. |
| `9110 12-15` | informative | A client sending Accept: */*;q=0 to solicit a 406 must still be able to handle a different response, since the server may ignore the preference. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | they still need to be able to handle a different response since the server is allowed to ignore their preference | Never hard-fail on a response whose media type falls outside the sent Accept ranges; deliver it to the caller. |
| `9110 12-16` | informative | Grammar: Accept is a #-list of media-range with optional weight; media-range is */*, type/*, or type/subtype, followed by parameters. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Accept = #( media-range [ weight ] ) media-range = ( "*/*" / ( type "/" "*" ) / ( type "/" subtype ) ) parameters | Emit Accept as a comma-separated list of media-ranges, each optionally carrying media-type parameters and a trailing weight; accept the same shape when parsing response Accept. |
| `9110 12-17` | informative | "*/*" matches all media types and "type/*" matches all subtypes of that type; a media-range may carry applicable media type parameters. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The asterisk "*" character is used to group media types into ranges, with "*/*" indicating all media types and "type/*" indicating all subtypes of that type. | Support both wildcard forms in any Accept construction/matching helper; type/* must not be confused with a literal subtype named '*'. |
| `9110 12-18` | SHOULD | Senders using weights in Accept should place the "q" parameter last, after all media-range parameters. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Senders using weights SHOULD send "q" last (after all media-range parameters). | When serializing Accept, always append ;q=... after any media-type parameters of the range. |
| `9110 12-19` | SHOULD | Recipients should treat any parameter named "q" as the weight regardless of its position among parameters. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Recipients SHOULD process any parameter named "q" as weight, regardless of parameter ordering. | Accept parser (for response Accept fields) must recognize q as weight even if followed by other parameters. |
| `9110 12-20` | informative | When multiple media ranges apply to a type, the most specific reference takes precedence. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If more than one media range applies to a given type, the most specific reference has precedence. | If implementing media-range matching (e.g., evaluating a server's response Accept), order precedence: type/subtype;params > type/subtype > type/* > */*. |
| `9110 12-21` | informative | The quality factor for a media type is the weight of the highest-precedence media range that matches it. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The media type quality factor associated with a given type is determined by finding the media range with the highest precedence that matches the type. | In any range-matching helper, pick the weight from the most specific matching range, not the first or highest weight. |
| `9110 12-22` | informative | Grammar: Accept-Charset is a #-list of (token or "*") each with optional weight. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Charset = #( ( token / "*" ) [ weight ] ) | If emitting Accept-Charset, each member must be a charset token or "*", optionally followed by ;q=weight. |
| `9110 12-23` | MAY | A user agent may attach a quality value to each charset in Accept-Charset to express relative preference. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | A user agent MAY associate a quality value with each charset to indicate the user's relative preference for that charset | Support optional per-charset weights in the Accept-Charset builder; not required. |
| `9110 12-24` | informative | In Accept-Charset, "*" matches every charset not mentioned elsewhere in the field. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The special value "*", if present in the Accept-Charset header field, matches every charset that is not mentioned elsewhere in the field. | Treat "*" as a fallback matching only unmentioned charsets (explicit entries take precedence) in any evaluation logic. |
| `9110 12-25` | informative | Accept-Charset is deprecated; general-purpose user agents do not send it unless specifically configured to. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Charset is deprecated because UTF-8 has become nearly ubiquitous and sending a detailed list of user-preferred charsets wastes bandwidth | Never emit Accept-Charset by default; only when explicitly set by the caller. |
| `9110 12-26` | informative | Accept-Encoding in a request states which content codings are acceptable in the response. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | When sent by a user agent in a request, Accept-Encoding indicates the content codings acceptable in a response. | Only advertise content codings (gzip, br, ...) the client can actually decode; the sent list is a promise of decode capability. |
| `9110 12-27` | informative | Accept-Encoding in a response states which content codings the server prefers in subsequent request content to that resource. <br>_(server,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | When sent by a server in a response, Accept-Encoding provides information about which content codings are preferred in the content of a subsequent request to the same resource. | Do not treat Accept-Encoding in a response as an error; optionally expose it so callers can choose request-body codings. |
| `9110 12-28` | informative | The "identity" token in Accept-Encoding is a synonym for "no encoding". <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An "identity" token is used as a synonym for "no encoding" in order to communicate when no encoding is preferred. | Recognize "identity" as a valid codings token meaning unencoded content, both when emitting and evaluating; it is not a real content coding to apply. |
| `9110 12-29` | informative | Grammar: Accept-Encoding is a #-list of codings with optional weight; codings is content-coding, "identity", or "*". <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Encoding = #( codings [ weight ] ) codings = content-coding / "identity" / "*" | Serialize Accept-Encoding per this ABNF; note #-list permits an empty field value (see 12-36). |
| `9110 12-30` | MAY | Each codings value in Accept-Encoding may be given an associated quality value expressing preference for that encoding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Each codings value MAY be given an associated quality value (weight) representing the preference for that encoding | Support optional per-coding weights (e.g., gzip;q=1.0, br;q=0.8) in the Accept-Encoding builder. |
| `9110 12-31` | informative | In Accept-Encoding, "*" matches any available content coding not explicitly listed in the field. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The asterisk "*" symbol in an Accept-Encoding field matches any available content coding not explicitly listed in the field. | Treat "*" as covering only unlisted codings; explicit entries override it in any evaluation. |
| `9110 12-32` | informative | If the request has no Accept-Encoding field, the server considers any content coding acceptable to the user agent. <br>_(server,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | If no Accept-Encoding header field is in the request, any content coding is considered acceptable by the user agent. | Critical: a client that omits Accept-Encoding must be prepared to receive ANY coding; if decode support is limited, always send Accept-Encoding listing supported codings. |
| `9110 12-33` | informative | An unencoded representation is acceptable by default unless the request's Accept-Encoding excludes it via "identity;q=0" or "*;q=0" with no more specific identity entry. <br>_(server,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | it is acceptable by default unless specifically excluded by the Accept-Encoding header field stating either "identity;q=0" or "*;q=0" without a more specific entry for "identity" | Know that unencoded responses stay acceptable regardless of the coding list sent; only identity;q=0 or *;q=0 forbids them — support emitting these forms if the caller wants that. |
| `9110 12-34` | informative | A content coding listed in Accept-Encoding is acceptable unless it carries qvalue 0. <br>_(server,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | If the representation's content coding is one of the content codings listed in the Accept-Encoding field value, then it is acceptable unless it is accompanied by a qvalue of 0 | Support emitting exclusions like gzip;q=0; when evaluating a response Accept-Encoding, treat q=0 entries as forbidden codings. |
| `9110 12-35` | informative | Among acceptable same-purpose content codings, the one with the highest non-zero qvalue is preferred. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the acceptable content coding with the highest non-zero qvalue is preferred | Express relative coding preference via distinct qvalues; expect the server to pick the highest non-zero one. |
| `9110 12-36` | informative | An Accept-Encoding field with an empty value means the user agent wants no content coding in the response. <br>_(user-agent,server [CLIENT])_ | An Accept-Encoding header field with a field value that is empty implies that the user agent does not want any content coding in response. | Allow the caller to send an empty-valued Accept-Encoding to request an unencoded response; the emitter must not reject or drop the empty value. |
| `9110 12-37` | SHOULD | If no available representation matches a non-empty Accept-Encoding, the origin server should respond without any content coding unless identity is marked unacceptable. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server SHOULD send a response without any content coding unless the identity coding is indicated as unacceptable | Even after sending Accept-Encoding, always be able to consume an unencoded (identity) response body. |
| `9110 12-38` | informative | Accept-Encoding received in a response is evaluated using the same rules as in a request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The field value is evaluated the same way as in a request. | Reuse the same Accept-Encoding parsing/evaluation code for response instances (request content negotiation) as for building requests. |
| `9110 12-39` | informative | Servers failing a request due to an unsupported content coding ought to send 415 and include an Accept-Encoding field, letting clients distinguish coding from media-type problems. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Servers that fail a request due to an unsupported content coding ought to respond with a 415 (Unsupported Media Type) status and include an Accept-Encoding header field in that response | On 415 WITH Accept-Encoding: interpret as request-body coding rejection; retry logic may resend with an acceptable (or no) coding. |
| `9110 12-40` | MUST NOT | Servers must not include Accept-Encoding in a 415 response whose cause is unrelated to content codings. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | servers that fail a request with a 415 status for reasons unrelated to content codings MUST NOT include the Accept-Encoding header field | Server-only emission rule; client may rely on it: 415 WITHOUT Accept-Encoding means media-type problem — do not retry with a different content coding. |
| `9110 12-41` | informative | Grammar: Accept-Language is a #-list of language-range with optional weight; language-range is defined by RFC 4647 Section 2.1. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Language = #( language-range [ weight ] ) language-range = <language-range, see [RFC4647], Section 2.1> | Emit Accept-Language members as RFC 4647 language-ranges (e.g., da, en-gb, *) each with optional ;q=weight. |
| `9110 12-42` | informative | Listing order of language tags as descending priority is a behavior of some recipients that cannot be relied upon. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | However, this behavior cannot be relied upon. | Do not depend on member order to convey priority in Accept-Language; encode priority in qvalues. |
| `9110 12-43` | informative | For interoperability, user agents commonly assign each language tag a unique quality value and list tags in decreasing quality order. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | many user agents assign each language tag a unique quality value while also listing them in order of decreasing quality | When the caller supplies an ordered language list, generate unique descending qvalues (e.g., 1, 0.9, 0.8) and preserve the order. |
| `9110 12-44` | MUST NOT | A user agent that does not give the user control over linguistic preferences must not send an Accept-Language field. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | A user agent that does not provide such control to the user MUST NOT send an Accept-Language header field. | Never emit Accept-Language by default; send it only when explicitly configured by the caller (caller configuration constitutes the required user control). |
| `9110 12-45` | informative | Vary in a response names the parts of the request, other than method and target URI, that may have influenced content selection. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | describes what parts of a request message, aside from the method and target URI, might have influenced the origin server's process for selecting the content of this response | Parse Vary from responses and expose it; it is the signal for which request fields determined the representation. |
| `9110 12-46` | informative | Grammar: Vary is a #-list whose members are "*" or field-name. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Vary = #( "*" / field-name ) | Vary parser must accept a comma-separated list of field names and/or the literal "*". |
| `9110 12-47` | informative | Selecting header fields listed in Vary are not limited to fields defined by this specification. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Potential selecting header fields are not limited to fields defined by this specification. | Vary parser must accept arbitrary/unknown field names, not just a known-header whitelist. |
| `9110 12-48` | informative | When Vary contains "*", a recipient cannot determine whether the response is appropriate for a later request without contacting the origin server. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient will not be able to determine whether this response is appropriate for a later request without forwarding the request to the origin server. | Treat Vary: * responses as non-reusable for any equivalence/dedup logic without revalidation at the origin. |
| `9110 12-49` | MUST NOT | A proxy must not generate "*" in a Vary field value. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MUST NOT generate "*" in a Vary field value. | Proxy-only; irrelevant to a pure client. If a forwarding/proxy mode is ever added, never synthesize Vary: *. |
| `9110 12-50` | MUST NOT | Cache recipients must not use a response with Vary-listed fields to satisfy a later request unless that request has the same values for those fields (or reuse is validated by the origin). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | they MUST NOT use this response to satisfy a later request unless the later request has the same values for the listed header fields as the original request | Cache-only; poseidon has no response cache. If one is added, the cache key must incorporate the values of all Vary-listed request fields. |
| `9110 12-51` | informative | Vary informs user agents that the response was subject to content negotiation and different values in the listed fields may yield a different representation. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | this response was subject to content negotiation (Section 12) and a different representation might be sent in a subsequent request if other values are provided in the listed header fields | Expose Vary to callers as the indicator that re-requesting with different listed header values can produce a different representation. |
| `9110 12-52` | SHOULD | An origin server should generate Vary on a cacheable response it wants selectively reused for subsequent requests. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server SHOULD generate a Vary header field on a cacheable response when it wishes that response to be selectively reused for subsequent requests. | Origin-only emission rule; nothing for the client to implement — but do not assume Vary is always present on negotiated responses (it may be elided). |

### RFC 9110 13 - Conditional Requests

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9110 13-1` | informative | If-Match makes the request method conditional on the origin server having any current representation (value "*") or one whose entity tag matches a listed tag. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | makes the request method conditional on the recipient origin server either having at least one current representation of the target resource, when the field value is "*" | When emitting If-Match, send "*" or a list of entity tags previously received in ETag for the target resource. |
| `9110 13-2` | MUST | Origin servers must use the strong comparison function when comparing entity tags for If-Match. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | An origin server MUST use the strong comparison function when comparing entity tags for If-Match | Comparison is server-side, but the client should never place weak (W/) tags in If-Match: under strong comparison they can never match, so the request always fails with 412. |
| `9110 13-3` | informative | Grammar: If-Match is either "*" or a comma-separated list of entity-tags. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If-Match = "*" / #entity-tag | Serializer must emit If-Match as exactly "*" or a #entity-tag list; never mix, never emit an empty list. |
| `9110 13-4` | MUST | An origin server receiving If-Match on a representation-selecting request must evaluate the condition per Section 13.2 before performing the method. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | the origin server MUST evaluate the If-Match condition per Section 13.2 prior to performing the method | Server-side; no client code path. |
| `9110 13-5` | informative | If-Match evaluation algorithm: "*" is true if any current representation exists; a tag list is true if any tag matches the selected representation; otherwise false. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | If the field value is "*", the condition is true if the origin server has a current representation for the target resource. | Server-side algorithm; informs client expectation that a stale tag yields 412. |
| `9110 13-6` | MUST NOT | An origin server must not perform the requested method when the If-Match condition evaluates to false. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server that evaluates an If-Match condition MUST NOT perform the requested method if the condition evaluates to false. | Server-side; client may assume the method did not run when it receives 412. |
| `9110 13-7` | MAY | On a false If-Match condition the origin server may respond 412 (Precondition Failed). <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server MAY indicate that the conditional request failed by responding with a 412 (Precondition Failed) status code. | Client must recognize 412 as precondition failure and not blindly retry without refreshing its validator. |
| `9110 13-8` | MAY | If the state-changing request appears already applied, the origin server may instead respond 2xx to a failed If-Match. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | if the request is a state-changing operation that appears to have already been applied to the selected representation, the origin server MAY respond with a 2xx (Successful) status code | Client must tolerate a 2xx to a conditional state-change even when its validator was stale (change already applied); do not treat as protocol error. |
| `9110 13-9` | MAY | A client may send If-Match on a GET to prefer a 412 over a new representation (range-completion use case). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY send an If-Match header field in a GET request to indicate that it would prefer a 412 (Precondition Failed) response if the selected representation does not match. | Optional API surface: permit If-Match on GET; document that If-Range is usually the better tool for range requests. |
| `9110 13-10` | MAY | A cache or intermediary may ignore If-Match. <br>_(cache,proxy [not-client])_ | A cache or intermediary MAY ignore If-Match because its interoperability features are only necessary for an origin server. | Client cannot rely on intermediaries enforcing If-Match; only the origin evaluates it. |
| `9110 13-11` | informative | An If-Match list containing "*" together with other members (or multiple "*") is syntactically invalid and not allowed to be generated. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | an If-Match header field with a list value containing "*" and other values (including other instances of "*") is syntactically invalid (therefore not allowed to be generated) | Emitter must never produce If-Match: *, "tag" or repeated "*"; validate before serializing. |
| `9110 13-12` | informative | If-None-Match makes the method conditional on the recipient NOT having any current representation ("*") or the selected representation's tag not matching any listed tag. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | makes the request method conditional on a recipient cache or origin server either not having any current representation of the target resource, when the field value is "*" | Emit If-None-Match with stored ETags for cache revalidation, or "*" as a create-only guard. |
| `9110 13-13` | MUST | Recipients must use the weak comparison function when comparing entity tags for If-None-Match. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST use the weak comparison function when comparing entity tags for If-None-Match | Client may include weak tags (W/"...") verbatim in If-None-Match — they are valid for cache validation under weak comparison. |
| `9110 13-14` | informative | Grammar: If-None-Match is either "*" or a comma-separated list of entity-tags. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If-None-Match = "*" / #entity-tag | Serializer must emit If-None-Match as "*" or a #entity-tag list (weak W/ tags allowed). |
| `9110 13-15` | SHOULD | A client updating stored responses that have entity tags should generate If-None-Match listing those tags on the GET request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client SHOULD generate an If-None-Match header field containing a list of those entity tags when making a GET request | If the client caches responses with ETags, build If-None-Match from all stored tags on revalidation GETs so the server can answer 304. |
| `9110 13-16` | informative | If-None-Match: * can be used to prevent an unsafe method (e.g., PUT) from modifying an existing representation when the client believes none exists. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If-None-Match can also be used with a value of "*" to prevent an unsafe request method (e.g., PUT) from inadvertently modifying an existing representation of the target resource | Support If-None-Match: * on PUT (create-only semantics) in the client API. |
| `9110 13-17` | MUST | An origin server receiving If-None-Match on a representation-selecting request must evaluate the condition per Section 13.2 before performing the method. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | the origin server MUST evaluate the If-None-Match condition per Section 13.2 prior to performing the method | Server-side; no client code path. |
| `9110 13-18` | informative | If-None-Match evaluation algorithm: "*" is false if a current representation exists; a list is false if any tag matches; otherwise true. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | If the field value is "*", the condition is false if the origin server has a current representation for the target resource. | Server-side; informs client expectation of 304/412 on any (weak) match. |
| `9110 13-19` | MUST NOT | An origin server must not perform the requested method when the If-None-Match condition evaluates to false. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server that evaluates an If-None-Match condition MUST NOT perform the requested method if the condition evaluates to false | Server-side; client may assume the method did not run on 304/412. |
| `9110 13-20` | MUST | On a false If-None-Match, the origin server must respond 304 for GET/HEAD or 412 for all other methods. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server MUST respond with either a) the 304 (Not Modified) status code if the request method is GET or HEAD or b) the 412 (Precondition Failed) status code | Client must handle 304 on conditional GET/HEAD (serve/refresh stored response, no body) and 412 on other methods. |
| `9110 13-21` | informative | An If-None-Match list containing "*" together with other members (or multiple "*") is syntactically invalid and not allowed to be generated. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | an If-None-Match header field with a list value containing "*" and other values (including other instances of "*") is syntactically invalid (therefore not allowed to be generated) | Emitter must never produce If-None-Match: * mixed with other members; validate before serializing. |
| `9110 13-22` | informative | If-Modified-Since makes only a GET or HEAD request conditional on the representation's modification date being more recent than the field value. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The "If-Modified-Since" header field makes a GET or HEAD request method conditional on the selected representation's modification date being more recent than the date provided in the field value. | Only attach If-Modified-Since to GET or HEAD requests; it is meaningless (and ignored) elsewhere. |
| `9110 13-23` | informative | Grammar: If-Modified-Since carries a single HTTP-date. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If-Modified-Since = HTTP-date | Serializer must emit exactly one valid HTTP-date (IMF-fixdate) as the field value. |
| `9110 13-24` | MUST | A recipient must ignore If-Modified-Since when the request also contains If-None-Match. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST ignore If-Modified-Since if the request contains an If-None-Match header field | If the client sends both, expect only If-None-Match to be evaluated; IMS is merely a legacy-intermediary fallback. |
| `9110 13-25` | MUST | A recipient must ignore If-Modified-Since if the value is not a valid HTTP-date, has more than one member, or the method is not GET/HEAD. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST ignore the If-Modified-Since header field if the received field value is not a valid HTTP-date, the field value has more than one member, or if the request method is neither GET nor HEAD. | Client must emit exactly one syntactically valid HTTP-date and only on GET/HEAD, or the header will be silently ignored (full 200 returned). |
| `9110 13-26` | MUST | A recipient must ignore If-Modified-Since if the resource has no modification date available. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | A recipient MUST ignore the If-Modified-Since header field if the resource does not have a modification date available. | Server-side; client gets a full 200 when the resource lacks Last-Modified. |
| `9110 13-27` | MUST | A recipient must interpret the If-Modified-Since timestamp in terms of the origin server's clock. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST interpret an If-Modified-Since field value's timestamp in terms of the origin server's clock. | Generate the IMS value from the server's own timestamps (prior Last-Modified or Date), not the client's local clock. |
| `9110 13-28` | SHOULD | An origin server receiving If-Modified-Since without If-None-Match should evaluate the condition per Section 13.2 before performing the method. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server SHOULD evaluate the If-Modified-Since condition per Section 13.2 prior to performing the method | Only SHOULD-strength: client cannot rely on getting 304 even when the resource is unchanged; must always handle full 200. |
| `9110 13-29` | informative | If-Modified-Since evaluation: false when the representation's last modification date is earlier than or equal to the field value; otherwise true. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | If the selected representation's last modification date is earlier or equal to the date provided in the field value, the condition is false. | Server-side; note equality counts as unmodified, so an exact echo of Last-Modified yields 304. |
| `9110 13-30` | SHOULD NOT | An origin server should not perform the requested method when the If-Modified-Since condition is false. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server that evaluates an If-Modified-Since condition SHOULD NOT perform the requested method if the condition evaluates to false | Server-side. |
| `9110 13-31` | SHOULD | On a false If-Modified-Since the origin server should generate a 304 response containing only metadata useful for identifying or updating a cached response. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server SHOULD generate a 304 (Not Modified) response, including only those metadata that are useful for identifying or updating a previously cached response | Client must accept a bodiless 304 with sparse headers and merge them into its stored response rather than expecting full metadata. |
| `9110 13-32` | informative | If-Unmodified-Since makes the request method conditional on the representation's last modification date being earlier than or equal to the field value. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The "If-Unmodified-Since" header field makes the request method conditional on the selected representation's last modification date being earlier than or equal to the date provided in the field value. | Emit If-Unmodified-Since from the stored Last-Modified as a lost-update guard when no ETag is available. |
| `9110 13-33` | informative | Grammar: If-Unmodified-Since carries a single HTTP-date. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If-Unmodified-Since = HTTP-date | Serializer must emit exactly one valid HTTP-date as the field value. |
| `9110 13-34` | MUST | A recipient must ignore If-Unmodified-Since when the request also contains If-Match. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST ignore If-Unmodified-Since if the request contains an If-Match header field | If the client sends both, expect only If-Match to be evaluated; IUS is a legacy-intermediary fallback. |
| `9110 13-35` | MUST | A recipient must ignore If-Unmodified-Since if the value is not a valid HTTP-date, including when it appears to be a list of dates. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST ignore the If-Unmodified-Since header field if the received field value is not a valid HTTP-date (including when the field value appears to be a list of dates). | Client must emit a single valid HTTP-date; a malformed or list value silently disables the precondition. |
| `9110 13-36` | MUST | A recipient must ignore If-Unmodified-Since if the resource has no modification date available. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | A recipient MUST ignore the If-Unmodified-Since header field if the resource does not have a modification date available. | Server-side; the guard is void against resources without Last-Modified. |
| `9110 13-37` | MUST | A recipient must interpret the If-Unmodified-Since timestamp in terms of the origin server's clock. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient MUST interpret an If-Unmodified-Since field value's timestamp in terms of the origin server's clock. | Use the server's Last-Modified value verbatim; do not compute the date from the client's local clock. |
| `9110 13-38` | MUST | An origin server receiving If-Unmodified-Since without If-Match must evaluate the condition per Section 13.2 before performing the method. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | the origin server MUST evaluate the If-Unmodified-Since condition per Section 13.2 prior to performing the method | Server-side; no client code path. |
| `9110 13-39` | informative | If-Unmodified-Since evaluation: true when the last modification date is earlier than or equal to the field value; otherwise false. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | If the selected representation's last modification date is earlier than or equal to the date provided in the field value, the condition is true. | Server-side algorithm. |
| `9110 13-40` | MUST NOT | An origin server must not perform the requested method when the If-Unmodified-Since condition is false. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server that evaluates an If-Unmodified-Since condition MUST NOT perform the requested method if the condition evaluates to false. | Server-side; client may assume the method did not run on 412. |
| `9110 13-41` | MAY | On a false If-Unmodified-Since the origin server may respond 412 (Precondition Failed). <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | the origin server MAY indicate that the conditional request failed by responding with a 412 (Precondition Failed) status code. | Client must recognize 412 as precondition failure for date-guarded requests. |
| `9110 13-42` | MAY | If the state-changing request appears already applied, the origin server may respond 2xx to a failed If-Unmodified-Since. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | if the request is a state-changing operation that appears to have already been applied to the selected representation, the origin server MAY respond with a 2xx (Successful) status code | Client must tolerate 2xx even though the resource changed since its date (already-applied case). |
| `9110 13-43` | MAY | A client may send If-Unmodified-Since on a GET to prefer 412 if the representation has been modified. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY send an If-Unmodified-Since header field in a GET request to indicate that it would prefer a 412 (Precondition Failed) response if the selected representation has been modified. | Optional: allow IUS on GET for range-completion flows; If-Range is normally preferable. |
| `9110 13-44` | MAY | A cache or intermediary may ignore If-Unmodified-Since. <br>_(cache,proxy [not-client])_ | A cache or intermediary MAY ignore If-Unmodified-Since because its interoperability features are only necessary for an origin server. | Client cannot rely on intermediaries enforcing IUS. |
| `9110 13-45` | informative | If-Range instructs the recipient to ignore the Range field when the validator does not match, transferring the full new representation instead of 412. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | instructs the recipient to ignore the Range header field if the validator doesn't match, resulting in transfer of the new selected representation instead of a 412 (Precondition Failed) response | Implement If-Range for resumable downloads: matched validator yields 206 continuation, mismatch yields a full 200 body. |
| `9110 13-46` | informative | Grammar: If-Range carries exactly one entity-tag or one HTTP-date (never a list). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If-Range = entity-tag / HTTP-date | Serializer must emit a single entity-tag OR a single HTTP-date; reject lists. |
| `9110 13-47` | informative | A recipient distinguishes an entity-tag from an HTTP-date in If-Range by examining the first three characters for a DQUOTE. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A valid entity-tag can be distinguished from a valid HTTP-date by examining the first three characters for a DQUOTE. | Client must emit well-formed values (etag starting with DQUOTE or W/", or a valid HTTP-date) so recipients can disambiguate by the first three characters. |
| `9110 13-48` | MUST NOT | A client must not generate If-Range in a request that does not contain a Range header field. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT generate an If-Range header field in a request that does not contain a Range header field. | Enforce in the request builder: If-Range is only legal when the same request carries Range. |
| `9110 13-49` | MUST | A server must ignore an If-Range received in a request without a Range header field. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST ignore an If-Range header field received in a request that does not contain a Range header field. | Server-side; complements the client-side MUST NOT in 13-48. |
| `9110 13-50` | MUST | An origin server must ignore If-Range on a request for a target resource that does not support Range requests. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server MUST ignore an If-Range header field received in a request for a target resource that does not support Range requests. | Server-side; client must anyway be prepared for a full 200 instead of 206. |
| `9110 13-51` | MUST NOT | A client must not generate If-Range containing a weak entity tag. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT generate an If-Range header field containing an entity tag that is marked as weak. | Reject W/-prefixed tags when building If-Range; only strong ETags qualify. |
| `9110 13-52` | MUST NOT | A client must not generate an HTTP-date If-Range unless it has no entity tag for the representation and the date is a strong validator (Section 8.8.2.2). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT generate an If-Range header field containing an HTTP-date unless the client has no entity tag for the corresponding representation and the date is a strong validator | Prefer the ETag; fall back to Last-Modified only when no ETag exists AND the date qualifies as strong per 8.8.2.2. |
| `9110 13-53` | MUST | A server receiving If-Range on a Range request must evaluate the condition per Section 13.2 before performing the method. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server that receives an If-Range header field on a Range request MUST evaluate the condition per Section 13.2 prior to performing the method. | Server-side; no client code path. |
| `9110 13-54` | informative | HTTP-date If-Range evaluation: false unless the date is a strong validator AND exactly matches the selected representation's Last-Modified; otherwise false. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the HTTP-date validator provided is not a strong validator in the sense defined by Section 8.8.2.2, the condition is false. | Server-side; explains why the client must echo the stored Last-Modified exactly (13-58). |
| `9110 13-55` | informative | Entity-tag If-Range evaluation: true only if the tag exactly matches the selected representation's ETag under the strong comparison function. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the entity-tag validator provided exactly matches the ETag field value for the selected representation using the strong comparison function (Section 8.8.3.2), the condition is true. | Server-side; a weak tag in If-Range can never match, reinforcing 13-51. |
| `9110 13-56` | MUST | A recipient of If-Range must ignore the Range header field when the If-Range condition evaluates to false. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | A recipient of an If-Range header field MUST ignore the Range header field if the If-Range condition evaluates to false. | Client must handle a full 200 response where it hoped for 206, discarding/replacing its partial copy. |
| `9110 13-57` | SHOULD | When the If-Range condition is true, the recipient should process the Range header field as requested. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | Otherwise, the recipient SHOULD process the Range header field as requested. | Client can expect 206 with the requested ranges when the validator matches, but must still tolerate 200 (SHOULD-strength only). |
| `9110 13-58` | informative | If-Range comparison is by exact match, including for HTTP-dates, unlike the earlier-than-or-equal comparison of If-Unmodified-Since. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | the If-Range comparison is by exact match, including when the validator is an HTTP-date | Echo the received Last-Modified value byte-for-byte in If-Range; any reformatting or recomputed date breaks the exact match. |
| `9110 13-59` | MUST | A recipient cache or origin server must evaluate preconditions after its normal request checks succeed and just before processing content or performing the method. <br>_(cache,origin [not-client])_ | a recipient cache or origin server MUST evaluate received request preconditions after it has successfully performed its normal request checks and just before it would process the request content | Server-side ordering rule. |
| `9110 13-60` | MUST | A server must ignore all preconditions when the unconditional response would have been anything other than 2xx or 412. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MUST ignore all received preconditions if its response to the same request without those conditions, prior to processing the request content, would have been a status code other than a 2xx (Successful) or 412 (Precondition Failed). | On 3xx/4xx/5xx (redirect, auth, etc.) the client must not conclude anything about its preconditions — they were never evaluated. |
| `9110 13-61` | MUST NOT | A server that is neither the origin nor able to act as a cache for the target resource must not evaluate the conditional header fields. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A server that is not the origin server for the target resource and cannot act as a cache for requests on the target resource MUST NOT evaluate the conditional request header fields | Proxy-only rule. |
| `9110 13-62` | MUST | Such a non-origin, non-cache server must forward the conditional header fields when it forwards the request. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | it MUST forward them if the request is forwarded | Proxy-only; conditionals pass through intermediaries untouched so the client's fields reach the origin. |
| `9110 13-63` | MUST | A server must ignore the conditional request header fields on methods that do not involve selecting or modifying a representation (e.g., CONNECT, OPTIONS, TRACE). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | MUST ignore the conditional request header fields defined by this specification when received with a request method that does not involve the selection or modification of a selected representation | Do not attach conditional headers to CONNECT/OPTIONS/TRACE requests — they will be ignored; the client may warn or strip them. |
| `9110 13-64` | informative | Sending a conditional HEAD is pointless: a successful response is about the same size as a 304 and more useful than a 412. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | there is no point in sending a conditional HEAD because a successful response is around the same size as a 304 (Not Modified) response | Avoid generating conditional HEAD requests even though they are technically allowed. |
| `9110 13-65` | MUST | A recipient cache or origin server must evaluate preconditions in the fixed order: If-Match, then If-Unmodified-Since, then If-None-Match, then If-Modified-Since, then If-Range. <br>_(cache,origin [CLIENT])_ | A recipient cache or origin server MUST evaluate the request preconditions defined by this specification in the following order | Knowing the fixed order lets the client predict which of several conditionals produced a 412/304/206 and which fields were masked (If-Match masks IUS; If-None-Match masks IMS). |
| `9110 13-66` | informative | Step 5 of the precedence algorithm: on GET with Range and If-Range, a true condition with applicable Range yields 206; otherwise Range is ignored and 200 is returned. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | if true and the Range is applicable to the selected representation, respond 206 (Partial Content) | Client sending Range+If-Range on GET must branch on 206 (append partial content) vs 200 (replace with full new representation). |
| `9110 13-67` | informative | Extensions defining additional conditional request header fields ought to define their evaluation order relative to the fields defined here. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Any extension to HTTP that defines additional conditional request header fields ought to define the order for evaluating such fields in relation to those defined in this document | Spec-authoring guidance; no client code path unless custom conditional fields are added. |

### RFC 9110 14 - Range Requests (RFC 9110: range units, Range, Accept-Ranges, Content-Range, partial PUT, multipart/byteranges)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `9110 14-1` | OPTIONAL | Range requests are an optional HTTP feature; recipients that do not implement them may respond as to a normal GET. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Range requests are an OPTIONAL feature of HTTP | Client may implement Range, but must always tolerate a full 200 response to any range request. |
| `9110 14-2` | informative | A range unit name is syntactically a token. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | range-unit = token | Parse/emit range-unit as an HTTP token; reject non-token units in Accept-Ranges/Content-Range parsing. |
| `9110 14-3` | informative | Range unit names are compared case-insensitively. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | All range unit names are case-insensitive and ought to be registered within the "HTTP Range Unit Registry" | Match "bytes", "none", etc. case-insensitively when parsing Accept-Ranges and Content-Range. |
| `9110 14-4` | informative | A ranges-specifier is a range unit, "=", and a comma-separated list of one or more range-specs (int-range, suffix-range, or other-range). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | ranges-specifier = range-unit "=" range-set range-set = 1#range-spec range-spec = int-range / suffix-range / other-range | Emit Range values as unit "=" 1#range-spec; range-set uses the RFC 9110 #list rule (OWS around commas). |
| `9110 14-5` | informative | An int-range is first-pos "-" with optional last-pos, both 1*DIGIT. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | int-range = first-pos "-" [ last-pos ] first-pos = 1*DIGIT last-pos = 1*DIGIT | Emit int-ranges exactly as DIGITs-hyphen-[DIGITs]; no spaces inside a range-spec, no signs. |
| `9110 14-6` | informative | An int-range with last-pos present and less than first-pos is invalid. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An int-range is invalid if the last-pos value is present and less than the first-pos. | Validate before sending: never emit e.g. bytes=500-499; treat such a spec as invalid when parsing. |
| `9110 14-7` | informative | A suffix-range is "-" followed by a suffix-length of 1*DIGIT. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | suffix-range = "-" suffix-length suffix-length = 1*DIGIT | Emit last-N-bytes requests as "-N" with N in decimal digits only. |
| `9110 14-8` | informative | other-range is one or more VCHARs excluding comma, for extension range units. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | other-range = 1*( %x21-2B / %x2D-7E ) ; 1*(VCHAR excluding comma) | If supporting extension units, restrict other-range specifiers to VCHAR minus comma (comma is the range-set delimiter). |
| `9110 14-9` | informative | A ranges-specifier is invalid if any contained range-spec is invalid or undefined for its range-unit. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A ranges-specifier is invalid if it contains any range-spec that is invalid or undefined for the indicated range-unit. | Whole-specifier validation: one bad range-spec invalidates the entire Range value the client would emit. |
| `9110 14-10` | informative | A valid ranges-specifier is satisfiable if at least one contained range-spec is satisfiable per the range-unit; otherwise unsatisfiable. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A valid ranges-specifier is "satisfiable" if it contains at least one range-spec that is satisfiable, as defined by the indicated range-unit. | Understand server semantics: any one satisfiable range-spec is enough to expect 206 rather than 416. |
| `9110 14-11` | informative | The bytes range unit uses only int-range and suffix-range, never other-range. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Byte ranges do not use the other-range specifier. | For bytes=..., emit only int-range/suffix-range forms; reject other-range syntax under the bytes unit. |
| `9110 14-12` | informative | In bytes ranges, first-pos and last-pos are inclusive offsets and byte offsets start at zero. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | the byte positions specified are inclusive. Byte offsets start at zero. | Compute ranges zero-based and inclusive: first 500 bytes is bytes=0-499; length of an incl-range is last-pos - first-pos + 1. |
| `9110 14-13` | informative | Byte ranges apply to the content-coded (encoded) byte sequence, not the decoded data. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | each byte range is calculated with respect to the encoded sequence of bytes, not the sequence of underlying bytes that would be obtained after decoding. | When resuming a gzip-coded (Content-Encoding) download, offsets refer to the encoded stream; do not compute offsets over decoded data. |
| `9110 14-14` | informative | A bytes int-range with absent last-pos, or last-pos >= representation length, means the remainder of the representation. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the last-pos value is absent, or if the value is greater than or equal to the current length of the representation data, the byte range is interpreted as the remainder of the representation | Expect the 206 Content-Range last-pos to be clamped to length-1 even when the request asked beyond the end; open-ended bytes=N- is valid. |
| `9110 14-15` | informative | A suffix-range longer than the representation yields the entire representation. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the selected representation is shorter than the specified suffix-length, the entire representation is used. | After bytes=-N, be prepared to receive the whole representation (as 206 with first-pos 0) when N exceeds its length. |
| `9110 14-16` | informative | For GET, a valid bytes range-spec is satisfiable if it is an int-range with first-pos below the current length, or a suffix-range with non-zero suffix-length. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a valid bytes range-spec is satisfiable if it is either: an int-range with a first-pos that is less than the current length of the selected representation or a suffix-range with a non-zero suffix-length. | Predict 416 vs 206: bytes=-0 and int-ranges starting at/after the known length are unsatisfiable; avoid emitting them when length is known. |
| `9110 14-17` | informative | For a zero-length representation, the only satisfiable GET range-spec is a suffix-range with non-zero suffix-length. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | When a selected representation has zero length, the only satisfiable form of range-spec in a GET request is a suffix-range with a non-zero suffix-length. | Do not expect 206 for int-ranges against empty representations; only bytes=-N (N>0) is satisfiable there. |
| `9110 14-18` | MUST | Recipients must handle arbitrarily large decimal position/length values without integer-overflow parsing errors. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | recipients MUST anticipate potentially large decimal numerals and prevent parsing errors due to integer conversion overflows. | Parse first-pos/last-pos/suffix-length/complete-length with explicit overflow detection (e.g. checked uint64 accumulation); fail the value cleanly, never wrap. |
| `9110 14-19` | informative | The Range request header field value is a single ranges-specifier. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Range = ranges-specifier | Emit exactly one ranges-specifier as the Range field value, e.g. Range: bytes=0-499. |
| `9110 14-20` | MAY | A server is permitted to ignore the Range header field entirely. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY ignore the Range header field. | Client must handle a 200 response with the full representation to any ranged GET and not treat it as an error. |
| `9110 14-21` | informative | Origin servers and intermediate caches are advised to support byte ranges when possible. <br>_(origin,cache [not-client])_ | origin servers and intermediate caches ought to support byte ranges when possible | Server/cache-side advice; nothing for the client to implement. |
| `9110 14-22` | MUST | A server must ignore Range on a request method that is unrecognized or has no defined range handling. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MUST ignore a Range header field received with a request method that is unrecognized or for which range handling is not defined. | Do not send Range on non-GET requests expecting partial behavior; if sent, expect it to be ignored. |
| `9110 14-23` | informative | In this specification, GET is the only method with defined range handling. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | For this specification, GET is the only method for which range handling is defined. | Client API should only attach Range to GET requests (Range on other methods has no defined effect). |
| `9110 14-24` | MUST | An origin server must ignore a Range field whose range unit it does not understand. <br>_(origin [CLIENT])_ | An origin server MUST ignore a Range header field that contains a range unit it does not understand. | When sending a non-bytes range unit, be prepared for a full 200 response rather than 206 or 416. |
| `9110 14-25` | MAY | A proxy may discard a Range field whose range unit it does not understand. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MAY discard a Range header field that contains a range unit it does not understand. | Proxy-only; client just cannot rely on exotic range units surviving intermediaries. |
| `9110 14-26` | MAY | A range-supporting server may ignore or reject a Range field with an invalid ranges-specifier, more than two overlapping ranges, or many small non-ascending ranges. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | MAY ignore or reject a Range header field that contains an invalid ranges-specifier, a ranges-specifier with more than two overlapping ranges, or a set of many small ranges that are not listed in ascending order | **VERIFY** Never emit overlapping or unordered multi-range sets; handle both 200 (ignored) and outright rejection for malformed Range values. -- Quote omits an inline cross-reference present in the source. RFC reads: "MAY ignore or reject a Range header field that contains an invalid ranges-specifier (Section 14.1.1), a ranges-specifier with more than two overlapping ranges, or a set of many small ranges that are not listed in ascending order". The dropped "(Section 14.1.1)" is neither line-wrap nor whitespace, so the quote does not appear verbatim. Level (MAY) and applies_to (server) are otherwise correct. \|\| Quote drops the internal citation and is not verbatim. RFC reads: "MAY ignore or reject a Range header field that contains an invalid ranges-specifier (Section 14.1.1), a ranges-specifier with more than two overlapping ranges, or a set of many small ranges that are not listed in ascending order". The parenthetical "(Section 14.1.1)" after "invalid ranges-specifier" is missing, so the quoted string does not appear contiguously. Level (MAY) and applies_to (server) are correct. |
| `9110 14-27` | SHOULD NOT | A client should not request multiple ranges that are less efficient than one encompassing range. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client SHOULD NOT request multiple ranges that are inherently less efficient to process and transfer than a single range that encompasses the same data. | Coalesce adjacent/overlapping ranges into a single range before emitting a multi-range Range value. |
| `9110 14-28` | MAY | A range-supporting server may ignore Range when the selected representation has zero-length content. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server that supports range requests MAY ignore a Range header field when the selected representation has no content | Handle a 200 empty-body response to a ranged GET against an empty resource. |
| `9110 14-29` | SHOULD | A client requesting multiple ranges should list them in ascending order unless a later part is specifically needed first. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client that is requesting multiple ranges SHOULD list those ranges in ascending order | Sort the range-set ascending by first-pos when emitting multi-range requests, unless the caller explicitly needs out-of-order retrieval. |
| `9110 14-30` | informative | Range is evaluated after precondition fields and only when the non-ranged result would be 200; it is ignored when a conditional GET yields 304. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Range is ignored when a conditional GET would result in a 304 (Not Modified) response. | When combining Range with If-None-Match/If-Modified-Since, expect a bodyless 304 (no partial content) when the precondition selects 304. |
| `9110 14-31` | informative | If-Range can be used as a precondition gating application of the Range field. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The If-Range header field (Section 13.1.5) can be used as a precondition to applying the Range header field. | For resume-after-interrupt, send If-Range with the stored validator so a changed representation yields 200 full content instead of a mismatched 206. |
| `9110 14-32` | SHOULD | When preconditions pass and a supported, satisfiable ranges-specifier is received, the server should send 206 with the corresponding partial representation(s). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the server SHOULD send a 206 (Partial Content) response with content containing one or more partial representations that correspond to the satisfiable range-spec(s) requested. | Implement full 206 handling: single-part (Content-Range header + partial body) and multi-part (multipart/byteranges) responses. |
| `9110 14-33` | informative | A 206 does not imply all requested ranges were sent; a server may send only a portion first. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The above does not imply that a server will send all requested ranges. | After a multi-range request, compare received parts against requested ranges and re-request missing portions rather than assuming completeness. |
| `9110 14-34` | SHOULD | When the ranges-specifier is valid but the unit is unsupported or the specifier unsatisfiable, the server should send 416. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the server SHOULD send a 416 (Range Not Satisfiable) response. | Handle 416: do not retry the same range blindly; use the 416 Content-Range complete-length to recompute a valid range. |
| `9110 14-35` | informative | Accept-Ranges is a comma-separated list of one or more range units. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Ranges = acceptable-ranges acceptable-ranges = 1#range-unit | Parse Accept-Ranges as a #list of tokens; tolerate multiple units and list syntax, not just the literal "bytes". |
| `9110 14-36` | MAY | A client may issue range requests even without having seen Accept-Ranges. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY generate range requests regardless of having received an Accept-Ranges field. | Do not gate range-request capability on prior Accept-Ranges observation; it is advisory only. |
| `9110 14-37` | MUST NOT | A client must not assume Accept-Ranges guarantees future range requests will get partial responses. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a client MUST NOT assume that receiving an Accept-Ranges field means that future range requests will return partial responses | Even after seeing Accept-Ranges: bytes, every ranged GET must still handle a 200 full-body response path. |
| `9110 14-38` | MAY | A server not supporting range requests for the resource may send Accept-Ranges: none, a reserved unit advising clients not to attempt ranges. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server that does not support any kind of range request for the target resource MAY send Accept-Ranges: none | Recognize the reserved unit "none" (case-insensitive) and suppress subsequent range attempts on that request path. |
| `9110 14-39` | MAY | Accept-Ranges may appear in a trailer section, though a header field is preferred. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The Accept-Ranges field MAY be sent in a trailer section, but is preferred to be sent as a header field | If the client surfaces trailers, accept Accept-Ranges appearing there as well as in headers. |
| `9110 14-40` | informative | Content-Range is a range unit, SP, then either incl-range "/" (complete-length or "*"), or "*/" complete-length. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Range = range-unit SP ( range-resp / unsatisfied-range ) range-resp = incl-range "/" ( complete-length / "*" ) incl-range = first-pos "-" last-pos unsatisfied-range = "*/" complete-length complete-length = 1*DIGIT | Implement a Content-Range parser covering both range-resp (with "*" length) and unsatisfied-range ("*/N") forms, single SP after the unit. |
| `9110 14-41` | MUST NOT | A recipient of a 206 whose Content-Range unit it does not understand must not recombine it with a stored representation. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | If a 206 (Partial Content) response contains a Content-Range header field with a range unit that the recipient does not understand, the recipient MUST NOT attempt to recombine it with a stored representation. | **VERIFY** Before merging a 206 into a partially downloaded file, verify the Content-Range unit is one the client understands (bytes); otherwise discard/treat as opaque. -- Quote omits an inline cross-reference. RFC reads: "...contains a Content-Range header field with a range unit (Section 14.1) that the recipient does not understand, the recipient MUST NOT attempt to recombine it with a stored representation." The dropped "(Section 14.1)" is not a whitespace/line-wrap diff, so the quote is not verbatim. Level (MUST NOT) and applies_to (client, cache) are otherwise correct. \|\| Quote drops the internal citation and is not verbatim. RFC reads: "If a 206 (Partial Content) response contains a Content-Range header field with a range unit (Section 14.1) that the recipient does not understand, the recipient MUST NOT attempt to recombine it with a stored representation." The parenthetical "(Section 14.1)" after "range unit" is missing, so the quoted string does not appear contiguously. Level (MUST NOT) and applies_to (client/cache) are correct. |
| `9110 14-42` | SHOULD | A proxy receiving a 206 with an ununderstood range unit should forward it downstream. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy that receives such a message SHOULD forward it downstream. | Proxy-only forwarding rule. |
| `9110 14-43` | informative | Content-Range may be sent as a request modifier for partial PUT only under private client-origin agreements. <br>_(client,origin [CLIENT])_ | Content-Range might also be sent as a request modifier to request a partial PUT, as described in Section 14.5, based on private agreements between client and origin server. | Only emit Content-Range on PUT when the caller has an explicit prior agreement with the origin; never by default. |
| `9110 14-44` | MUST | A server must ignore Content-Range in a request whose method has no defined Content-Range support. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST ignore a Content-Range header field received in a request with a method for which Content-Range support is not defined. | Server-side rule; client takeaway is that request Content-Range outside PUT-by-agreement has no defined effect. |
| `9110 14-45` | SHOULD | For byte ranges, a sender should include the complete representation length unless it is unknown or hard to determine. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | For byte ranges, a sender SHOULD indicate the complete length of the representation from which the range has been extracted, unless the complete length is unknown or difficult to determine. | If the client ever emits Content-Range (partial PUT), include "/complete-length" when known; when parsing, prefer the length but tolerate its absence. |
| `9110 14-46` | informative | "*" in place of complete-length means the representation length was unknown when the field was generated. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An asterisk character ("*") in place of the complete-length indicates that the representation length was unknown when the header field was generated. | Parse bytes N-M/* as valid; do not derive total size from a "*" length. |
| `9110 14-47` | informative | A Content-Range value is invalid if last-pos < first-pos, or if complete-length <= last-pos. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A Content-Range field value is invalid if it contains a range-resp that has a last-pos value less than its first-pos value, or a complete-length value less than or equal to its last-pos value. | Validate received Content-Range: require first-pos <= last-pos and (when numeric) complete-length > last-pos; flag violations as invalid. |
| `9110 14-48` | MUST NOT | The recipient of an invalid Content-Range must not recombine the received content with a stored representation. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | The recipient of an invalid Content-Range MUST NOT attempt to recombine the received content with a stored representation. | On failed Content-Range validation (14-47), abort resume/merge logic; do not splice the body into a partial download. |
