| `9110 17-5` | informative | User agents can reduce phishing impact by prominently distinguishing or rejecting userinfo in URIs. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | by prominently distinguishing (or rejecting) userinfo when present | Reject or strip a userinfo component in target URIs before use (aligns with Section 4.2.4's prohibition on sending userinfo); never emit it on the wire or into Host. |
| `9110 17-6` | informative | User agents can reduce phishing impact by not sending stored credentials and cookies when the referring document comes from an unknown or untrusted source. <br>_(user-agent [not-client])_ | by not sending stored credentials and cookies when the referring document is from an unknown or untrusted source | Only applicable if the client ever grows a cookie jar or automatic credential store; poseidon currently has neither. Revisit if credential caching is added. |
| `9110 17-7` | informative | Implementers need to consider privacy and security implications of design, coding, and the configuration options (especially defaults) they expose. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Implementers need to consider the privacy and security implications of their design and coding decisions, and of the configuration options they provide to operators | Ship secure defaults: TLS verification on, header/size limits on, no permissive fallbacks unless the caller opts in explicitly. |
| `9110 17-8` | informative | An origin server needs to avoid accessing file-system names with special OS significance when mapping the target resource to files. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | an origin server needs to avoid accessing names that have a special significance to the system when mapping the target resource to files, folders, or directories | Server-only (path traversal / device names); no client action. |
| `9110 17-9` | informative | Data received in a request cannot be trusted by the server. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | data received in a request cannot be trusted | Server-side injection concern. Client-side analog (treat all peer response bytes as hostile) is already the repo's Peer-input policy; the binding text for that is RFC 9112 Section 11. |
| `9110 17-10` | informative | Resource implementations ought to avoid using request data in contexts processed or interpreted as instructions (command/code/SQL injection). <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | resource implementations ought to avoid use of request data in contexts that are processed or interpreted as instructions | Server-only; no client action. |
| `9110 17-11` | informative | Received data not based on fixed parameters ought to be carefully filtered or encoded before use. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | Received data that isn't based on fixed parameters ought to be carefully filtered or encoded to avoid being misinterpreted | Server-only; client analog is sanitizing response data before passing to logs/metrics, which is application-layer. |
| `9110 17-12` | informative | HTTP parsers are vulnerable to very long or very slow streams of data, particularly for protocol elements with no predefined length. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | parsers are often vulnerable to attacks based on sending very long (or very slow) streams of data | Enforce both size caps AND read deadlines when parsing server responses: bound status line, header block, chunk-size line, trailer section; support per-read timeouts so a slow-drip server cannot pin a connection forever. |
| `9110 17-13` | informative | Section 5.4's minimum size limits on fields are floor recommendations; most implementations are expected to choose substantially higher limits. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | specific recommendations are made for minimum size limits on fields (Section 5.4) | Client must support field lines of at least the Section 5.4 recommended minimum (8000 octets combined per field line) while still imposing a configurable upper bound on total header-section size. |
| `9110 17-14` | informative | A server can reject requests with a too-long target URI (414) or too-large request content (413). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server can reject a message that has a target URI that is too long (Section 15.5.15) or request content that is too large (Section 15.5.14) | Client must handle 414 and 413 responses gracefully (surface to caller, do not retry unchanged); avoid generating pathologically long request-targets. |
| `9110 17-15` | informative | Recipients ought to carefully limit processing of protocol elements including methods, response status phrases, field names, numeric values, and chunk lengths to prevent overflow and DoS. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Recipients ought to carefully limit the extent to which they process other protocol elements, including (but not limited to) request methods, response status phrases, field names, numeric values, and chunk lengths | Core client checklist item: cap reason-phrase length, field-name/value lengths and count, and parse all numeric elements (Content-Length, chunk-size) with explicit overflow rejection — never unchecked integer accumulation. Maps to the repo's bound-by-construction policy in http1. |
| `9110 17-16` | informative | The most effective mitigation against compression side-channel attacks (e.g., BREACH) is disabling compression on sensitive data or strictly separating it from attacker-controlled data. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The most effective mitigation for this risk is to disable compression on sensitive data, or to strictly separate sensitive data from attacker-controlled data | Do not enable TLS-level compression; if the client ever adds automatic request-body compression, make it opt-in per request so callers can exclude sensitive payloads. |
| `9110 17-17` | informative | Implementations need to prevent unintentional disclosure of the personal information clients hold. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | Implementations need to prevent unintentional disclosure of personal information | Never emit caller-supplied credentials/headers to unintended destinations (e.g., strip Authorization on cross-origin redirect); avoid dumping full header sets including auth material into logs, hooks, or error strings by default. |
| `9110 17-18` | informative | Server log information needs to be securely stored and analyzed under appropriate guidelines. <br>_(server,proxy [not-client])_ | Log information needs to be securely stored and appropriate guidelines followed for its analysis | Server/intermediary operational concern; no client protocol action. |
| `9110 17-19` | informative | Access traces keyed to a specific client are unsafe to publish even when the key is pseudonymous. <br>_(server,proxy [not-client])_ | access traces that are keyed to a specific client are unsafe to publish even if the key is pseudonymous | Operator concern for server-side logs; no client action. |
| `9110 17-20` | informative | Log information ought to be purged of personally identifiable information (user identifiers, IP addresses, user-provided query parameters) as soon as it is no longer operationally necessary. <br>_(server,proxy [not-client])_ | log information ought to be purged of personally identifiable information, including user identifiers, IP addresses, and user- provided query parameters, as soon as that information is no longer necessary | Server-side; optional hygiene for any client-side request logging the library's hooks enable, but not a protocol requirement on the client. |
| `9110 17-21` | informative | It is unwise to place sensitive or personally identifiable information inside a URI, since URIs are routinely displayed, logged, and shared. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | It is therefore unwise to include information within a URI that is sensitive, personally identifiable, or a risk to disclose | Documentation/API-design item: warn callers against secrets in query strings; ensure the client's own logging/metrics can redact target URIs. |
| `9110 17-22` | informative | Implementations mapping field names to interface names (e.g., CGI-style hyphen-to-underscore) are advised to make the mapping unambiguous and complete for all possible name octets. <br>_(server,gateway [not-client])_ | implementations that perform such mappings are advised to make the mapping unambiguous and complete for the full range of potential octets received as a name | Server/gateway smuggling concern (Transfer_Encoding vs Transfer-Encoding); client parser should still reject field names outside the token grammar, but that rule is RFC 9110 Section 5.1 / RFC 9112, not here. |
| `9110 17-23` | informative | A site using personal information in fragments ought to include a (possibly empty) fragment on redirects to other sites to block fragment inheritance. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | it ought to ensure that redirects to other sites include a (possibly empty) fragment component in order to block that inheritance | Origin-side; the client-side fragment-inheritance behavior on Location is defined in Section 10.2.2 and should be checked there, not here. |
| `9110 17-24` | informative | User-Agent, Via, and Server header fields often reveal information about the sender's software, aiding attackers in theory. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The User-Agent (Section 10.1.5), Via (Section 7.6.3), and Server (Section 10.2.4) header fields often reveal information about the respective sender's software systems | Make the client's User-Agent value caller-configurable and keep the default minimal (product/version only, no OS/system detail). |
| `9110 17-25` | informative | Proxies serving as firewall portals ought to take special precautions with header information that might identify hosts behind the firewall. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | Proxies that serve as a portal through a network firewall ought to take special precautions regarding the transfer of header information that might identify hosts behind the firewall | Proxy-only; no client action. |
| `9110 17-26` | informative | The Via header field permits intermediaries to replace sensitive machine names with pseudonyms. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | The Via header field allows intermediaries to replace sensitive machine names with pseudonyms | Intermediary-only allowance; client must merely tolerate pseudonyms in received Via values (parsing rule lives in Section 7.6.3). |
| `9110 17-27` | informative | A privacy-preserving approach is for a user agent to omit Accept-Language except for explicitly permitted sites. <br>_(user-agent [not-client])_ | omit the sending of Accept-Language except for sites that have been explicitly permitted | General-purpose-UA suggestion; satisfied trivially because the library never auto-injects Accept-Language — keep it that way. |
| `9110 17-28` | informative | In proxy-enhanced-privacy environments, user agents ought to be conservative in sending proactive negotiation header fields. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | user agents ought to be conservative in sending proactive negotiation header fields | Do not emit Accept, Accept-Charset, Accept-Encoding, or Accept-Language by default; send only what the caller explicitly sets (Accept-Encoding only if the client transparently decodes). |
| `9110 17-29` | informative | General-purpose user agents with highly configurable header fields ought to inform users of the privacy loss from providing too much detail. <br>_(user-agent [not-client])_ | General-purpose user agents that provide a high degree of header field configurability ought to inform users about the loss of privacy that might result if too much detail is provided | Browser-UI concern; at most a documentation note for library users. |
| `9110 17-30` | informative | User agents that cache representations ought to clear or replace the cache on privacy-maintaining user actions, since entity tags can be abused as persistent identifiers. <br>_(user-agent,cache [not-client])_ | User agents that cache representations ought to ensure that the cache is cleared or replaced whenever the user performs privacy-maintaining actions | Applies only if the client ever adds a representation cache with validator reuse; poseidon has none. Revisit if caching is added. |
| `9110 17-31` | informative | Servers ought to ignore, coalesce, or reject egregious range requests such as more than two overlapping ranges or many small out-of-order ranges. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Servers ought to ignore, coalesce, or reject egregious range requests, such as requests for more than two overlapping ranges or for many small ranges in a single set | Server-side DoS defense; client-side corollary is to avoid emitting overlapping/many-small multi-range requests, since servers may legitimately ignore or reject them. |
| `9110 17-32` | informative | Services depending on individual user authentication require a secured connection before credentials are exchanged. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | Services that depend on individual user authentication require a secured connection prior to exchanging credentials | Client checklist: send user credentials (Authorization etc.) only over https/TLS connections by default; sending them over cleartext http should require explicit caller opt-in. |
| `9110 17-33` | informative | User agents that cache credentials are encouraged to provide a readily accessible, user-controlled mechanism for discarding them. <br>_(user-agent [not-client])_ | User agents that cache credentials are encouraged to provide a readily accessible mechanism for discarding cached credentials under user control | Applies only if the client adds credential caching; poseidon does not cache credentials today. If added, expose an explicit clear/invalidate API. |
| `9110 17-34` | informative | Clients that authenticated to one resource can reuse the same credentials for other resources on the same origin server, which risks credential harvesting across a shared origin. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients that have successfully made authenticated requests with a resource can use the same authentication credentials for other resources on the same origin server | If the client ever auto-attaches cached credentials, scope reuse no wider than the same origin (and honor realm/protection-space rules of Section 11.6); never replay credentials cross-origin, including after redirects. |
| `9110 17-35` | informative | Mitigations for shared protection spaces include restricting direct access to Authorization content and separating parties by distinct host name or port. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | restricting direct access to authentication credentials (i.e., not making the content of the Authorization request header field available), and separating protection spaces by using a different host name (or port number) | Origin deployment concern; no client action. |
| `9110 17-36` | informative | Adding fields such as Authentication-Info to responses sent over an unencrypted channel can affect security and privacy by revealing that authentication is in use. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Adding information to responses that are sent over an unencrypted channel can affect security and privacy | Server and auth-scheme-definition concern; client merely parses these fields if present. |
